### Post description
> why does price stop dead at a level… and other days slice straight through everything?
>
> it’s gamma. and it changes through the day.
>
> options market makers hedge in futures, all day. turn their gamma on its side and it’s a map:
>
> positive gamma is a hill. they sell rallies and buy dips, so price climbs slow, stalls and rolls back.
> thin gamma is a flat field. nothing’s pushing, so price drifts to the next hill.
> negative gamma is ice. they hedge with the move, so price speeds up.
>
> the line between them is the flip. above it the day chops. slide under it and the same day runs.
>
> it’s never just a positive or negative day. it’s where price is on the map.
>
> hills stop price. ice makes it run. know the terrain.
>
> follow @kestradess for more.
>
> #orderflow #gammaexposure #nqfutures #daytrading #futurestrading optionsflow tradingeducation dealerhedging

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gamma / gamma exposure (GEX), gamma flip | concept | throughout | A standard options-market concept. Under the usual assumption that dealers are long customer calls and short customer puts, positive net gamma means dealers trade against moves (dampening) and negative net gamma means they trade with moves (amplifying). The "flip" is the price where net dealer gamma crosses zero. |
| 30,050 / 29,970–30,060 levels | numbers | steps 2–19 | Illustrative NQ-style prices. No date or real data. |
| @kestradess | handle | caption | Creator, Kesiena Isaac. |
| #nqfutures | hashtag | caption | Nasdaq-100 E-mini futures. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Only "follow @kestradess for more". The post has no link, comment keyword or price. The "Γσ"/"kt" branding suggests a personal brand that may later sell a gamma or order-flow product, but this post sells nothing.

### Verification & insights
- The mechanics described match mainstream GEX explanations (e.g. Option Alpha, LuxAlgo glossaries): positive gamma suppresses volatility and pins price, negative gamma amplifies moves, and the flip is the zero-gamma crossing.
- Caveats the video only partly states: GEX levels are model estimates built on assumptions about dealer positioning that nobody can observe directly. 0DTE flows change gamma intraday, and futures traders don't see SPX/NDX options gamma directly. The video does say "it won't hold every time".
- No data source, backtest or statistic is presented, so this is a conceptual explainer only.
- Not verified: who Kesiena Isaac is or any product behind the brand.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment 100K" triggers an automated DM (typical ManyChat-style) with an e-book. That builds his DM list and boosts engagement: the post had about 244 comments versus about 88 likes, which shows the comment-gate at work. Behind it, the account and the associated "Pro Tradingz" brand sell trading services. No price is shown in the post, and no affiliate link to Anthropic.

### Verification & insights
- **Sound parts:** select by robustness rather than return; include costs and slippage; out-of-sample, walk-forward and parameter-sensitivity checks; paper trade before live; treat the LLM as an assistant while code does the calculating. All standard best practice.
- **Weak or incorrect parts:**
  - The 250,000 figure is a multiplication error (it is 25,000).
  - Testing 25k–100k+ variants on the same history guarantees some will look great by chance (the multiple-testing / data-snooping problem). Simple OOS "PASS" flags do not fix this. You need corrections such as White's Reality Check, Hansen's SPA, the Deflated Sharpe Ratio or the Probability of Backtest Overfitting (Bailey & López de Prado). None are mentioned.
  - Walk-forward and OOS also get "used up" if you iterate on them repeatedly with an LLM in the loop.
  - The rule set is generic retail technical analysis. Combining many weak rules rarely creates a real edge.
- Free, better-known references: López de Prado, *Advances in Financial Machine Learning* (ch. 11–12 on backtest overfitting); Bailey et al., "The Probability of Backtest Overfitting" (SSRN 2326253); open-source frameworks such as backtesting.py, vectorbt and QuantConnect LEAN.
- Not verified: the e-book contents (deliberately not requested, to avoid the funnel), and Pro Tradingz offerings and pricing.

- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE: dealer gamma hedging is a real flow, and gamma-heavy strikes often act as magnets or pivots. "Markets move because of Gamma Exposure" and "the hedge is the order flow" overstate it, though. GEX is a model estimate built on assumptions about dealer positioning. The post is a lead funnel for a paid course whose price was not found.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- **Comment "MISS"** gets an auto-DM with the application form (lead and qualification capture) for a waitlist to **Gamma Exposure University**, the creator's paid options/GEX education programme. "Closed, but apply in case a spot opens" is a scarcity tactic. No price was found publicly. Application-gated programmes like this usually end in a sales call.

### Verification & insights
- **"Dealers... are required to hedge their Gamma Exposure on ES and NQ":** there is no requirement to use ES/NQ specifically. Market makers delta-hedge, and they may use the ETF itself, futures, or other instruments. Futures are a common, cheap hedge, so the idea is directionally reasonable but stated too strongly.
- **"Markets move because of Gamma Exposure" / "the futures moves are a downstream consequence of the hedge, not the cause":** an overclaim. Hedging flows are one input among many (macro news, institutional orders, CTAs and so on). Positive dealer gamma tends to dampen moves and negative gamma tends to amplify them, which is accepted market-microstructure thinking but not the sole cause.
- **"Reversals at strikes = dealers hedging":** partly true. Large open-interest strikes often pin or reverse price, but you cannot tell after the fact whether a given reversal came from hedging.
- GEX numbers come from models that assume dealers are short calls and long puts (or similar). Different vendors publish different values.
- The "PAPER TRADE / good habits" flash frame looks like an editing leftover.
- Could not verify: the course's price, its curriculum, or who runs it, beyond the account name "Nick Ireland".

- The post is brand content for **SBPro X (ClusterTick)**, a commercial charting platform with a 10-day free demo, then a paid licence (price not found), plus paid courses and a partner/affiliate programme. No link or comment keyword appears in the visible caption, which may be truncated.

### Verification & insights
- The concept is real and widely taught: a prior session's value area and POC often act as reference levels, and "breakout then retest of VAH/POC" is a common setup.
- **The claim "price often reacts... on the next trading day"** rests on one hand-picked example. No sample size, win rate, or failed cases (for example price slicing through POC) are shown. That is classic cherry-picking.

| Out-of-sample (OOS) test | method | audio 11.2s, prompt | Valid practice. A single OOS split is weak on its own (walk-forward or multiple periods are better), and if you re-tune after seeing OOS results it stops being out-of-sample. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- **Comment "ALGO"** gets a DM with a link to the "Astral" platform. Creators who promote a tool this way almost always use a referral/affiliate link or are partnered with the platform. The post discloses no sponsorship. The platform's price is unknown. Account stats when fetched: 129 likes, 27 comments (mostly "Algo" comments, presumably).

### Verification & insights
- The advice itself is mainstream and correct in spirit (include costs, test out of sample, paper trade first).
- Caveats:
  - Set fees to your actual venue's taker or maker rate, not a generic 5 bps.
  - OOS tests are only meaningful if the strategy wasn't iterated on the same data. "Just tell it to run an OOS test" through an AI chat doesn't guarantee a proper hold-out.
  - A Calmar near 12 with ~98% annualised return on a backtest is a red flag for overfitting or a short test window. The screen shows no date range.
- "Astral" could not be identified or verified, including who operates it, whether it holds customer funds or broker keys, and what it costs. Be cautious before connecting a brokerage or exchange API key.
- Could not verify: the platform, the affiliate terms, or the exact backtest figures (the screen is blurry).

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No explicit CTA beyond "follow for more". No link, product or price is shown in the post. The "deepcharts" tag suggests the visuals mimic DeepCharts-style gamma and footprint tools, but no affiliate code is shown. Web search found no product or course for @kestradess or "Kesiena Isaac". This looks like audience-building, possibly ahead of selling an indicator or community later (not confirmed).

### Verification & insights
- The core ideas are real. 0DTE options imply an expected daily range. Dealer gamma concentrations can act as magnets or pins. Footprint "absorption" is standard order-flow vocabulary.
- Overclaims:
  - "a lot more accurately than you" and "most days, NQ plays it out" come with no statistics.
  - Implied moves are priced to be wrong in both directions about equally often (with a typical variance premium). They don't reliably tell you "which way is easier".
  - Skew ("options lean") mostly reflects demand for downside protection, not a directional forecast.
- The chart is a hand-built animation with a perfect V-reversal. It is hindsight-shaped, with no costs, no losing example and no sample size.
- The post does honestly say "it won't make 100% of your trades winners."
- Could not verify: any track record of the creator, or how the "options lean" indicator is computed.

(3) Short on a break of its low (long on a break of its high in a down-stretch). (4) Stop just beyond the wick. (5) Target = VWAP. (6) Never enter on the exhaustion candle itself |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Commenting "EMT" sends an automated DM with the free checklist. The display name "Futures Trading Coach" shows the account sells coaching or mentorship. The checklist is the lead magnet that collects contacts for an upsell. No price or bio link was retrievable (web search found nothing on "G Street Trades"/Cody Ganske). Prop-firm content often carries affiliate discount codes for Apex; none is shown in this post.

### Verification & insights
- The setup itself is a legitimate, widely taught intraday mean-reversion fade (VWAP/EMA extension + reversal candle + break-of-candle trigger). Waiting for the break rather than entering on the exhaustion candle is sensible.
- Missing:
  - no win rate, no sample size, no losing examples
  - no market or ticker
  - no handling of trend days, when price stays stretched and "exhaustion" fails repeatedly. This is the classic way VWAP-fade strategies lose.
- "Passed over 60 prop firm evals" mostly reflects how cheap and repeatable Apex evaluations are (frequently discounted). Passing evaluations is not the same as sustained profitable trading. The visible payouts ($2,000 cap-sized amounts and $501) are modest relative to what 60 evaluation fees cost, and nothing shows net results.
- "LITERALLY RETIRE your 9 to 5" is an unsupported income claim.
- Could not verify: the 60-evals claim, total payouts, the checklist contents (blurred on screen, not requested), or any coaching price.

- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE – VWAP really is the dominant execution benchmark and the rules are standard intraday technique, but "front-run billions of dollars of flow" overstates any edge; no stats; CTA feeds a newsletter funnel

### Post description
> Billions in algo flow lean towards one line.
>
> That's the VWAP (volume weighted average price)
>
> The entry only exists after a qualified break.
>
> 1. Price breaks the opening range, the first 30 minutes.
> 2. The move extends at least one full range width past the break.
> 3. No close back through VWAP on the way.
>
> All three, then the first touch of the line is your entry. The algos that missed the leg are waiting there to fill.

### Audio
The creator speaks to camera at a whiteboard (about 340 words, so these are close timestamped notes with key lines quoted). No identifiable music.
- [0.0–10.4] "All institutional traders get paid to do one thing. Beat this line." Once you understand it, he claims, "you can front-run billions of dollars of flow and beat them at their own game."
- [10.4–23.8] Most sizable equity flow today is executed by algorithms, and their most common benchmark is the VWAP (volume-weighted average price), which any charting software will draw for you.
- [25.4–38.4] Example: when a pension fund calls JP Morgan to buy 10 million shares, the desk must fill at or better than VWAP. Beat the line and "you get a gold star"; miss it and you get "an awkward phone call from your client."
- [38.4–43.8] "So billions of dollars of flow is programmed to hug this line all session long."
- [46.6–55.4] **Rule 1:** know what VWAP is: "the session's fair price". Above it buyers are in control; below it sellers are.
- [56.5–80.3] **Rule 2:** "enter your long on the first pullback after a qualified break." Not every break qualifies; the three-part checklist is in the caption. When the break qualifies, the first VWAP touch is the long entry, because algos that missed the leg up "are waiting hungry to get the fill for their clients."
- [80.3–106.4] **Rule 3:** "acceptance below the VWAP means the regime has changed." On a five-minute chart, three consecutive closes below VWAP plus a retest of the line that holds means control has shifted, and you should adjust your position. "One stab through the VWAP doesn't mean anything. We need this confirmation."
- [106.4–115.9] Homework: open a five-minute chart, add VWAP and study historical price action to get comfortable before risking your own money.
- [115.9–126.9] "why draw your own lines that only you can see when you can trade the line that banks are paid to beat?"

(Whisper wrote "VWOP"; the on-screen subtitles spell it "VWAP".)

### On-screen content, page by page
All 79 pages are one continuous shot: the creator in a black polo at a whiteboard, with burned-in two-line subtitles that match the audio word for word. The listing below groups the pages by what changes.

**Pages 1–2 (0–4.4s):** Title label "Beating the **trading algos**". Subtitle "All institutional traders get paid to do one thing, beat this line." The whiteboard sketch: a wavy price line rising above a blue VWAP curve, a tall spike, a dotted horizontal line labelled "Break", and below it "First Touch" and a dashed "STOP" line.
**Pages 3–11 (4.4–18.9s):** Subtitles only, matching the audio 4.4–18.9s.

Any examples, tickers, or scenarios are illustrative only. Past performance doesn't guarantee future results."

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| VWAP | indicator / benchmark | caption, audio, pages 12–13 | Volume-weighted average price. It genuinely is the most common agency-execution benchmark (VWAP algos are standard broker products) |
| JP Morgan | company | audio 25.4s, page 18 | Used as an illustrative bank desk |
| Pension fund buying 10 million shares | example | audio 25–29s | Hypothetical |
| Opening range = first 30 minutes | rule parameter | caption item 1 | Standard opening-range-breakout definition |
| "extends at least one full range width past the break" | rule parameter | caption item 2 | The move must travel at least 1× the opening-range height beyond the breakout |
| "No close back through VWAP on the way" | rule parameter | caption item 3 | Trend-integrity filter. If all three hold, the first VWAP touch is the long entry |
| 5-minute chart; 3 consecutive closes below VWAP + a retest that holds | rule / settings | audio 88–96s | Regime-change / exit rule |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Commenting "LETTER" triggers an auto-DM with a link to the creator's market-analysis newsletter ("our full market analysis"). It is an email-list sign-up, likely free at entry with paid tiers or sponsorships behind it. Web search found no official newsletter domain or price, so its exact name and cost could not be verified. No product is sold in the video itself, and a standard disclaimer is shown.

### Verification & insights
- True: large institutional orders are commonly worked by VWAP algorithms and benchmarked to VWAP, and "above VWAP = buyers in control" is a common intraday heuristic.
- Overstated:
  - "front-run billions of dollars of flow" and "algos… waiting hungry to get the fill". VWAP algos spread their orders over the day according to volume curves; they don't specifically buy the first touch of the VWAP line.
  - Retail traders cannot see or "front-run" that flow. Front-running in the legal sense means trading ahead of known client orders, which is illegal for brokers. Here it is used loosely.
- The rules (ORB + pullback to VWAP + a 3-close regime filter) are reasonable discretionary technique, but the post gives no win rate, no stop/target rules beyond the sketch, no costs and no backtest. The creator himself advises studying historical charts before risking money.
- Could not verify: the newsletter's name and price (the caption says only "get our full market analysis on your desk").

## Depn6kbScDY | @optionsriddler | "Be the casino": text-card explainer claiming hedge funds/market makers sell options, collect theta, hedge delta-neutral, so retail should sell premium instead of buying calls
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/Depn6kbScDY/
- **Account:** @optionsriddler (display name not captured) · **Posted:** unknown (not in mirror data) · **Type:** reel (37.8s, 4 scene pages; ~13 distinct text cards from 2-fps frames)
- **Caption source:** possibly truncated (mirror). It is short and complete-looking (ends in hashtags).
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE – the variance risk premium and market-maker delta hedging are real, but it presents option selling as risk-free "house edge" and ignores tail risk, gamma risk and the fact that dealers earn the spread, not just theta

### Post description
> Be the casino and not the gambler! #investingforbeginners #daytrading #optionstrading

### Audio
A narrator voice-over (calm, possibly synthetic) over a soft background bed. No identifiable song.
- [0.0–9.5] "Hedge funds don't buy options, they sell them to you. Every time you buy a call betting on a moonshot, a market maker takes the other side and keeps your premium."
- [10.3–15.7] "You're renting time, they're collecting rent. Every day your option decays, theta pays them."
- [16.6–25.9] "Then they hedge away the direction, delta neutral, so they keep the premium whether the stock rises or falls. They don't predict, they don't gamble, they don't chase."
- [26.9–30.1] "Retail bets on direction, Wall Street gets paid by the clock."
- [30.9–37.8] "The edge was never the trade, it's being the house. Stop thinking like the gambler."

### On-screen content, page by page
Every card: a "BE THE CASINO" outlined label at top, a green progress bar with a countdown timer (0:37 → 0:02) at top right, "@optionsriddler" at bottom right, and one karaoke-style subtitle word line at the bottom.

1. (0–3s) "HEDGE FUNDS **DON'T BUY OPTIONS.** / **THEY SELL THEM — TO YOU.** / the other side of your trade". Subtitles "Hedge funds", "sell them".
2. (3–8s) Red box "YOU BUY THE CALL / betting on a moonshot"; green box "THEY KEEP YOUR PREMIUM / market maker takes the other side". Subtitles "a call", "the other".
3. (~10–15s) "YOU'RE RENTING TIME / THEY'RE COLLECTING **RENT** / Θ / every day your option decays, **theta pays them**". Subtitles "collecting rent.", "decays, theta".
4. (~16–23s) "THEN THEY HEDGE THE DIRECTION AWAY / [DELTA-NEUTRAL] / keep the premium whether the stock **RISES or FALLS**". Subtitles "the direction", "the premium".
5. (~23–26s) "THEY DON'T / Predict / Gamble / Chase / no predictions. no lottery tickets." Subtitle "don't gamble."
6. (~27–30s) Red box "RETAIL BETS ON DIRECTION"; green box "WALL STREET GETS PAID BY THE CLOCK / time is the edge". Subtitles "on direction.", "clock."
7. (~31–34s) "THE EDGE WAS NEVER THE TRADE. / IT'S BEING **THE HOUSE.** / the casino doesn't gamble". Subtitle "It's being".
8. (~35–37.8s) "STOP THINKING **LIKE THE GAMBLER.** / think like the casino / follow for more". Subtitle "Stop thinking".

(The four scene-change frames show the blank card between text fades. The text was read from the 2-fps frames.)

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Theta (Θ) | Greek | page 3, audio 10–15s | Time decay of option value; real |
| Delta-neutral hedging | concept | page 4, audio 16–21s | Real market-maker practice. It requires continuous rehedging and leaves gamma/vega exposure |
| Market maker / hedge funds | entities | pages 1–2 | Conflated. Market makers (e.g. Citadel Securities, Susquehanna) earn mainly the bid-ask spread. Many hedge funds both buy and sell options |
| "follow for more" | CTA | page 8 | Only CTA |
| #investingforbeginners #daytrading #optionstrading | hashtags | caption | — |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Nothing is sold in this post; the only CTA is "follow for more". The faceless template format (text cards, countdown, likely synthetic voice) is typical of accounts that build an audience for later course, Discord or affiliate (broker) offers. No bio link was available to check.

### Verification & insights
- Real: on average, option sellers have historically collected a variance risk premium (implied volatility tends to exceed realised), and most short-dated retail call buying loses money.
- Misleading framing:
  - Selling options is not "being the house" with a guaranteed edge. Short-premium strategies take small, frequent gains and occasional very large losses: short-vol blow-ups in Feb 2018 ("Volmageddon", XIV) and the collapse of options-selling fund LJM Partners in 2018 are well-known cases.
  - Retail traders cannot hedge delta-neutral cheaply. Dealers' profit comes largely from the spread and flow, not from just "keeping the premium".
  - Selling naked options needs high broker approval levels and margin, and carries unlimited risk on short calls.
- No numbers, backtests or products are named, so there is nothing further to verify.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment EDGE" triggers an auto-DM with the link. The site itself appears to be **London Strategic Edge** (londonstrategicedge.com): free, no card, free API key; no paid tier found on the pages fetched. The keyword "EDGE" matches the brand name. The account name "davidtrades_model" and the faceless screen-recording style suggest a promo or UGC account possibly tied to the site, but no disclosure was found. Owner/operator not identified; a job-board listing calls it a fintech startup with staff in Lahore, Pakistan.

### Verification & insights
- Web fetches of londonstrategicedge.com and /data confirmed the 118,000 datasets, free plan limits and ML/backtest features; no operator or company name is published.
- **PF 21.79 is not realistic as an edge.** 73 trades over 5.5 years, with entry/exit rules not shown and possibly optimized on the same data, means a profit factor of about 21 is almost certainly an overfit or look-ahead artifact. The narrator admits "It's a random example". Real robust strategies typically show a PF of 1.2–2. Costs beyond a 1.5-pip spread, slippage and out-of-sample testing are not shown.
- "Open source" here means free to use; no source code repo was found.
- Free alternatives with known provenance: FRED, Yahoo Finance via yfinance, Stooq, Dukascopy tick data.
- Could not verify: the 30+ years / 500+ TB claims, data quality, and who runs the site.

| Social Science Research Network (SSRN.com) | site | audio 7.8s, whiteboard | Real, free preprint repository (owned by Elsevier); its Financial Economics Network hosts many strategy/anomaly papers |

Full episode: "4 Institutional Scalping Strategies Market Makers Keep SECRET (Automate Prop Firm Trading)" |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No CTA beyond "Follow @chartfanatics". The clip promotes the full YouTube episode and the Chart Fanatics brand, which makes money from trading-education content and prop-firm/broker partnerships. The guest's episodes centre on algos for passing prop-firm challenges. No price shown, and no course for the guest found.

### Verification & insights
- The SSRN claim is directionally reasonable (quant desks do mine academic papers) but the 90% figure is rhetorical. The famous caution: many published anomalies decay after publication (McLean & Pontiff, 2016, "Does Academic Research Destroy Stock Return Predictability?") and many fail replication (Hou, Xue & Zhang, "Replicating Anomalies").
- The title "THIS Made Him BILLIONS" is clickbait; nothing shows billions made from SSRN ideas.
- Useful free alternatives along the same lines: SSRN FEN, arXiv q-fin, Quantpedia's free strategy summaries.
- Could not verify: the guest's track record, or the post date.

- **Verdict:** PARTLY TRUE: the filter settings are real Unusual Whales features and a common way to screen for "opening, directional" flow. Several explanations overstate what the filters prove ("ask = buying", "Volume > OI = opening", deep-ITM buying = "arbitrage"). The tool is paid (about $42–120/month), and following large flow is not a proven edge.

### Post description
> These are my filters in UnusualWhales for finding options to trade 👆I'll be sharing my beginner guide on Selling Puts for Premium soon. Stay tuned.

### Audio
The creator's own voice-over (English), with burned-in subtitles. No song is identifiable. Condensed to timestamped notes:

- [21.5–32.8] Step 3: set all instrument types green except **indices**, since indices can be traded for many reasons and you'll never know even if flagged bullish or bearish; "Only focus on stocks, ETFs and ADRs." (Whisper heard "Or those focus on stocks ETFs and ADRs".)
- [32.8–50.8] Minimum premium **$45,000**, so you see orders over $45,000. A single one needn't get attention, but several $45[k] orders in the same ticker are "something to pay attention to".
- [50.8–64.7] "These are the most important next steps": **volume greater than OI**, which he says ensures you see opening trades; if OI exceeds volume they could be buying to close.
- [64.7–81.1] **Out of the money only**, so "they're buying something that hasn't happened yet". Buying deep in the money could be "committing arbitrage" (getting a discount, or using less cash to own the equity); OTM shows they expect a move.
- [81.1–97.8] **Single leg only** must be on: with multi-leg trades you can't tell a bullish from a bearish spread; single-leg means they are straight up buying the put or call, so "You know their position."

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- There is no comment-for-DM call to action. The post implicitly promotes the paid Unusual Whales subscription. No affiliate code or link is visible in the caption or on screen.
- The caption teases a coming "beginner guide on Selling Puts for Premium", which could become a free post or a paid guide. Nothing is priced yet.
- The bio and link for @4c_ema could not be retrieved (Instagram rate limit, HTTP 429).

### Verification & insights
- **Checked:** Unusual Whales exists, and its pricing was checked by web search. Unusual Whales' own Substack explains the filters: "Ask" side means likely buys, "volume > oi" sets Vol/OI to 1, and there is an "opening trade only" on/off switch. Its guides also exclude ITM contracts that "may not give a directional bias due to hedging or arbitrage".
- **Claims vs reality:**
  - "Ask side tells you if it's bullish or bearish": only partly true. Ask-side fills are probably buys, but a bought call can hedge a short stock position and a bought put can hedge a long one. Direction is inferred, not known.
  - "Volume > OI … ensures that they're buying to open": overstated. Volume above the previous day's OI means at least some contracts must be new, but it does not prove this particular print opened a position. The platform has a separate "Opening trades" toggle, which he left off.
  - "Buying deep ITM … you can be committing arbitrage": a misuse of the word. Deep-ITM buying is often stock replacement or hedging; the point that it has a weaker directional signal is valid.
  - "Single leg … you know their position": still not fully true. A single-leg print can be one leg of a multi-venue strategy or a hedge against stock.
  - "Multiple $45k orders in the same ticker": a sensible heuristic (repeated or aggregated flow), but no evidence of profitability is offered.
- **Realism:** Following unusual flow is a popular retail approach with mixed evidence. Large trades are often hedges, and there is no track record, win rate or backtest in this post.
- **Could not verify:** the creator's results, broker, and the content or price of the upcoming put-selling guide.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Comment 2026" triggers a DM with a "guide" to "which traders I copy". The likely monetization for a copy-trading funnel is a broker or copy-trading affiliate signup (referral link to a partner broker or signal service) or a paid Telegram group. The specific offer could not be confirmed without engaging. The account (Philipp L., German-language browser) has little web footprint.
- The masked, anonymous persona plus a large-balance screenshot is a common pattern in affiliate-driven trading accounts.

### Verification & insights
- **OpenInsider "before retail even notices":** Form 4 filings are public and are usually filed up to 2 business days *after* the trade. You see insider buys after insiders made them, at the same time as everyone else. Research shows insider *purchases* carry some modest information, but this is not front-running.
- **Glint "before major media":** news terminals aggregate fast sources (OSINT accounts, wires). Speed claims are vendor marketing, and pricing was not verifiable.
- **Myfxbook "verified track records… copy for free":** verification confirms the account data is real, but the top-ranked systems are survivorship-biased. Extreme gains like +426,963% usually mean tiny starting balances or martingale/high-risk strategies, and many blow up. Copying adds slippage and broker costs.
- **The "results" screenshot:** profit of 20,964.52 against 587,104.70 deposited (~3.6% over an unknown period) is not "every single week" evidence. The screenshot shows a few trades only. "PF Deduction" and the ".f" symbol suffix suggest a specific broker/prop setup, but no verification link is given. Treat it as unverified.
- **Not verified:** Glint's pricing and ownership; the content of the "2026" guide; any affiliate relationship.
- Sources: [Myfxbook AutoTrade review (daytrading.com)](https://www.daytrading.com/myfxbook), [Pepperstone Myfxbook page](https://next.stg.pretrade.pepperstone.com/en/trading-platforms/social-trading/myfxbook), [IC Markets](https://icmarkets.com/blog/?p=5457).

- **What you can give the AI:** trading rules, risk parameters, market-data tools, research tools, custom skills, and your own workflows.

### On-screen content, page by page

A Smarter Trader." and the footer "Educational content only. Not investment advice. Trading involves market risk." 1. TRADINGVIEW (Charts + Indicators + Alerts; "Alert Triggered – Condition met on NIFTY 50 – 12:15 PM").
2. WEBHOOK ("Sends alert to your endpoint"; Webhook Alert HTTP POST `{"symbol": "NIFTY", "price": 24781.50, "signal": "LONG", "time": "2024-11-19T12:15:00Z"}`; "Secure HTTPS endpoint from TradingView alerts").

4. YOUR ACTION ("Decide and act (your rules)": Get AI analysis – Trade idea + reasoning; Receive notification – Telegram / Email / Slack; Trigger automation (Optional, via your rules); Track & improve – Build a feedback loop).

- IMPORTANT: "Hermes does not automatically take over your account. You decide what it can do — from analysis and notifications to full automation (if you choose). Start simple. Keep it safe. Build and improve over time."

Quote: "Charts find the opportunities. AI helps you make better decisions." TradingView × HERMES AI AGENT, "SAME MARKETS. A SMARTER YOU."

**Slide 3 – "STEP 1: BUILD THE SIGNAL ON TRADINGVIEW." / "DON'T SEND THE WHOLE CHART. SEND STRUCTURED DATA."** Text: "Use TradingView's powerful charting tools and Pine Script to create your strategy and set an alert that sends a webhook with key information." The same NIFTY chart with a Publish button.

① EXAMPLE PINE SCRIPT ("Simple Moving Average Crossover with Webhook Alert."), excerpt reproduced as shown, including its errors:
```
//@version=6
indicator("Hermes Signal", overlay=true)
fast = ta.ema(close, 20)
slow = ta.ema(close, 50)
longSignal = ta.crossover(fast, slow)
shortSignal = ta.crossunder(fast, slow)
if longSignal
    alert('{"symbol":"' + syminfo.ticker +
        '","price":"!' + str.tostring(close) +
        '","signal":"LONG"}', alert.freq_once_per_bar_close)
```
The rest of the script (7 more lines) repeats the same alert for `shortSignal`, with `"signal":"SHORT"`, the same stray `"!` in the price field and `alert.freq_once_per_bar_close`. It then plots `fast` with `color=color.blue, title="EMA 20"` and `slow` with `color=color.red, title="EMA 50"`.
② ALERT PAYLOAD (EXAMPLE) "Structured data sent to your webhook endpoint": `{"symbol": ""NIFTY", "price": "24781.50", "signal": "LONG", "time": "2024-11-19T12:15:00Z", "interval": "15m"}`.
③ CREATE ALERT IN TRADINGVIEW: Condition "Hermes Signal (Long)" / "Any alert() function call", Expiration "Open-ended alert", Webhook URL "https://your-endpoint.com/webhook", Send webhook notification ✓, Send email notification ☐.
④ TEST YOUR ALERT: Create the alert; Use "Test Alert" button; Check your endpoint logs; Verify the JSON payload; Fix and iterate if needed.

PRO TIPS: Keep the payload simple and structured; Include only the data you need (symbol, price, signal, timeframe); Use clear signal logic to avoid false alerts; Test thoroughly before going live; You can add more fields like SL, TP, indicators, etc. Quote: "Good signals are the foundation. Everything else builds on this. — ARWAZ AZ".

- Flow: TradingView (Alert is triggered, e.g. EMA Crossover) → Webhook (HTTP POST, JSON payload) → HERMES AI AGENT (Receives the alert) → Analyzes with tools (Market context, Technical analysis, News / Events, Your trading rules) → Output (Analysis, decision or next steps).

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| TradingView | platform | all slides, caption | Real charting platform. Per TradingView support, webhook alerts need a paid plan (the free Basic plan has none) and 2FA, accept only ports 80/443, cancel requests that take over 3 s, and don't support IPv6. |
| Hermes / Hermes Agent | AI agent | all slides, caption | Nous Research's open-source agent (github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent, MIT, last commit 2026-10-20). Has a real webhook platform (`hermes webhook`, `hermes gateway setup`, default port 8644) and SKILL.md skills. Its webhooks **require an HMAC-SHA256 secret**; "INSECURE_NO_AUTH" is allowed on loopback only. |
| NIFTY 50 (NSE) | index | slides 1–6 | Indian equity index. All prices (O 24,781.50 …, EMA20 24,756.44, EMA50 24,712.36, SL 24,650, TP 25,050, confidence 72%) are illustrative mock values dated 2024-11-19, not a real trade record. |
| EMA 20 / EMA 50 crossover | strategy rule | slides 1, 3, 4, 5 | Long on a 20/50 EMA cross up, short on a cross down, 15-minute chart, alerts once per bar close. No backtest, costs or results are given. |
| Pine Script v6 example | code | slide 3 | Mostly valid structure. Bugs: it's labelled "Simple Moving Average" but uses `ta.ema`; the stray `"!` inside `"price":"!'` breaks the JSON; it omits the "time" and "interval" fields its own payload example shows; and the example payload has a doubled quote (`""NIFTY"`). |
| SKILL.md / config.yaml | config | slide 5 | Illustrative. The `triggers:` / `tools:` YAML keys shown are not Hermes's actual subscription schema (Hermes uses `hermes webhook subscribe` with prompt templates and skills). |
| Telegram / Email / Slack, MCP servers, broker API | integrations | slides 2, 4, 5, 6 | Generic options; no specific broker named |
| @arwazaz_ / Arwaz Az | creator | header | LinkedIn: Mohammad Arbaz Alam, a quant/algo-trading content creator from Bhagalpur, India |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment AGENT" plus "Save this post" is an engagement and DM-automation funnel. The "complete setup + resources" will most likely be a guide or link. No paid product, course, broker referral or price is visible in the slides or the full caption. Everything shown can be built from the free Hermes docs (skills/autonomous-ai-agents/hermes-agent/references/webhooks.md) and TradingView's webhook help page.

### Verification & insights
- I checked the existing clone scratchpad/repos/NousResearch__hermes-agent: LICENSE MIT; `gateway/platforms/webhook.py` says "secret (REQUIRED; 'INSECURE_NO_AUTH' skips validation, loopback only)". TradingView cannot add an HMAC signature, so a real setup needs a small relay (or a secret in the URL path plus a proxy) between TradingView and Hermes. The default port 8644 also has to sit behind a reverse proxy on 443. The slides don't mention any of this.
- An LLM agent can't answer within TradingView's 3-second limit, so the endpoint must acknowledge immediately and process asynchronously.
- No performance evidence is given. A plain 20/50 EMA crossover on NIFTY 15m is a well-known, whipsaw-prone signal. "Reduces false signals" and "Confidence: 72%" are unsupported; an LLM "confidence" number is not a calibrated probability.
- The safety framing is good: "Do NOT place trades unless explicitly allowed" and "No blind execution". Wiring the optional broker-API execution layer to an LLM is high-risk (prompt-injection via news or web search, hallucinated levels).
- Could not verify: what exactly the DM sends.

### Audio
The creator speaks to camera (English). Burned-in word-by-word subtitles. No identifiable music. Condensed into timestamped notes:
- [0.0–10.3] Hook: "What happens when you ask Claude to build you a trading bot and then let it trade your real money?" He says Claude tested over **26,000** ideas before it could touch a single dollar.
- [10.3–21.4] Step 1: ask Claude to gather **15 years of one-minute NASDAQ futures data** and build a backtester that accounts for **fees and slippage**.
- [21.4–25.6] Claim: "it literally built the whole testing lab with one prompt."
- [25.6–38.4] Step 2: in the same session, test the strategy on **2010 to 2020**, then on unseen years (**2020 to 2026**), and make sure it **beats a coin flip**.
- [38.4–45.3] Result: "almost every single strategy that [I] tested lost to a coin flip."
- [45.3–57.8] Step 3 ("a trick"): tell it to connect to your broker and place the trades, set a stop-loss [Whisper: "ATP"], and message you on **Discord** every time it executes a trade.
- [57.8–62.6] Claude builds the bot that trades and notifies you of entries.
- [62.6–73.1] "The best part": tell Claude to turn itself on at **nine in the morning every weekday**, give you a **kill button**, and stop for the day after each **$300** loss.
- [73.1–81.4] CTA: he will record a full in-depth tutorial on his **YouTube** "tonight"; follow him and check his YouTube.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- There is no comment-for-DM gate. The call to action is "follow me" plus "check out my YouTube" for a full tutorial, so the business model is audience growth and YouTube views.
- **Implicit promotion of Topstep / TopstepX.** The bot runs on a Topstep account. No affiliate code is visible here, but prop-firm creators commonly carry Topstep referral links. The bio could not be fetched (Instagram 429).
- No product or price is shown.

### Verification & insights
- **What "real money" means:** the account is a TopstepX account and the bot reports "Combine passed". The Trading Combine is a simulated evaluation that you pay a monthly fee for. Only a funded account after passing pays real (profit-split) money. "Let it trade my real money" is therefore at best loosely true.
- **The honest part:** the workflow shown (costs and slippage, in-sample/out-of-sample split, a random baseline) is textbook. "Almost every strategy lost to a coin flip" is a realistic finding, and that is unusual for this genre.
- **What is missing or misleading:**
  1. "Built the whole testing lab with one prompt": the chat shows only Claude's one-line acknowledgement, with no code, data source or results table.
  2. Testing 26,125 variants and then picking survivors on the "unseen" years turns 2020–2026 into a selection set. Without a further holdout or multiple-testing correction, survivors are likely flukes.
  3. No surviving strategy, rules, out-of-sample metrics or live P&L are shown. The $1,467 "profit lock" is a bot message, not a statement.
  4. Topstep's automation rules (own device, no VPS, prohibited strategies) are not mentioned; breaking them can void an account.
- **Risk:** futures with automated market orders and a server-side stop can still gap or slip. The $300 daily stop and kill switch are good ideas, but they are only spoken, not shown working.
- **Could not verify:** the creator's YouTube tutorial, any actual performance, and his bio link or affiliations (Instagram rate limit).

- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DesHQU6xTR8/

- **Verdict:** MISLEADING – the ICT session-sweep/BOS recipe is real and widely taught, but the "trade" is placed in TradingView **Bar Replay** (hindsight, simulated) and presented as "that easy". There are no results, no costs, and no win rate. The CTA is a lead magnet for a paid education funnel.

### Post description
> Inténtalo y me dices cómo te fue☺️

("Try it and tell me how it went ☺️")

### Audio
The creator speaks Spanish throughout (about 330 words), so these are close timestamped notes with key lines quoted. Whisper errors are corrected: "sista/bajista" = alcista/bajista, "reprimiento" = rompimiento, "gambugs" = Gann box, "ferbálico" = fair value gap.
- [0.0–4.0] "Lo que la gente te cobra 1.000 dólares, te lo voy a enseñar de gratis." (What people charge you $1,000 for, I'll teach you free.)
- [4.0–14.0] The video is "solamente para personas que sean de Puerto Rico y Estados Unidos" who trade or want to; anyone else should leave ("Vete de aquí… bye").
- [14.0–22.0] "Hola, mi nombre es Valeri y hago trading haciendo ICT concepts, específicamente en oro y en los índices, en futuros." (She trades ICT concepts on gold and index futures.)
- [22.0–26.0] This is part N of "cómo se coge un trade paso por paso" (how to take a trade, step by step).
- Step list as spoken:
  1. [26–35] On your chart go to **1-hour and 4-hour**. "Si está alcista, vas a buscar compras y si está bajista, vas a buscar ventas." (Bullish = look for buys; bearish = look for sells.)
  2. [35–38] Drop to **15 minutes**.
  3. [38–44] Mark the **Asia session** low and high.
  4. [44–50] Mark the **London session** low and high.
  5. [50–55] Mark **pre-market open at 8:30** and **the open at 9:30** (New York time).
  6. [55–60] "Si barren los bajos, vas a buscar compras y si barren los altos, vas a buscar ventas." (If the lows get swept, look for buys; if the highs get swept, look for sells.)
  7. [60–66] Trading an index: drop to **1 minute**. Trading gold: drop to **5 minutes**.
  8. [66–78] After the lows are swept, wait for a **break of structure**: "un cierre del cuerpo de la vela encima del último alto bajista" (a candle-body close above the last bearish high) confirms it.
  9. [78–89] Use the **Gann box** tool to measure the retracement, drawn from the last low to the last high. If it lines up with the break-of-structure zone, or there's a **fair value gap**, "you're doing it right".
  10. [89–98] Use the **Long position** tool. Entry at the **50% (equilibrium)**, or exactly at the line where structure broke.
  11. [98–105] Stop loss "un poquito más abajo" (a little below). Target the liquidity points marked earlier.
  12. [105–116] Click Trade, make sure the risk/reward is to your liking, click Buy, and wait.
- [116–128] "Y así de fácil se coge un trade… Hubiese cogido más profit, pero simplemente puedes ver lo fácil que fue." (That easy. Could have taken more profit, but you can see how easy it was.)
- [128–137] Follow for more trading content. "Estoy enviando una clase gratis a todas las personas que comenten VALUE aquí abajo." (Free class to everyone who comments VALUE.)

### On-screen content, page by page

Header "Micro E-mini Nasdaq-100 Index Futures · 1 · CME", O 29,707.75 H 29,735.5x L 29,707.75 C 29,725.25 +17.75; SELL 29,725.25 / BUY 29,725.25. ### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment VALUE" triggers an automated DM with a "free class". This is a standard lead magnet for trading-education sellers: a free webinar or video, then a paid mentorship, course or community, often paired with prop-firm affiliate links. Restricting the audience to "Puerto Rico and the United States" points to audience qualification for a paid offer or a US-only broker or prop-firm affiliate. No price, product name or bio link is visible in the material, and the account couldn't be found by web search.

### Verification & insights
- The trade is placed in **TradingView Bar Replay** (the "Replay" watermark and the "Select bar" controls are visible, dated Mon 19 Oct '26). The future candles were already known to the recording, so this demonstrates the mechanics, not live performance. "Así de fácil" is hindsight.
- The ICT sweep → BOS → 50% retrace entry is a real, popular discretionary setup. It has no published, audited edge. Its results depend on subjective choices (which high or low, which BOS, where exactly the stop goes).
- Not mentioned: commissions and fees on futures, slippage on 1-minute entries, and that $871 risk on 7 micro contracts requires an appropriately sized account.
- "Trading explained to a 5-year-old" is a hook; the content is a fairly technical ICT sequence.
- Could not verify: the account's real name, bio link, or what the "free class" leads to; the post date.

### Post description
> Over $100,000 in ONE MONTH using a day trading strategy built around just TWO indicators.
>

- VWAP: identifies market direction and potential entries. EMA9: confirms momentum and manages exits.
- EMA9 crossing **above** VWAP → looking for bullish momentum and CALL entries; crossing **below** → bearish momentum and PUT entries.
- 3-minute chart for direction, 1-minute chart to fine-tune entries.
- "Two indicators. One strategy. A repeatable process." The goal is to wait for the right setup and execute with discipline, not to trade more.
- Disclaimer: "These are my personal trading results, not typical or guaranteed returns. Trading involves substantial risk."

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Two layers. (1) "Follow + comment SETUP" sends a "free strategy" by DM (lead capture plus engagement boost; 80 comments, almost all "Setup"). (2) The real product is the **monthly membership via the bio link**: "trade LIVE with me every day", with a 7-day free trial that converts to a recurring charge. The price wasn't found in web search (the account and product aren't indexed). The caption includes an earnings disclaimer ("not typical or guaranteed").

### Verification & insights
- Read every value in the calendar (above). The sum is +$122K for Sep 1–22, so "over $100,000" is consistent with the image. The percentages show this is about +24% on a roughly $500K account. Nothing shows it came from this one strategy, from real money, or what was lost in other months. It's unverified (no broker statement).
- The two chart examples are picked after the fact on big trend days ("especially for trend days"). VWAP/EMA crossovers whipsaw repeatedly on range days, and he shows no range-day losses. "You would have been well over 100%" is hindsight on an option.
- Not mentioned: 0DTE SPX options carry high theta and gamma risk; a −20–30% premium stop can be gapped through; spreads and commissions apply.
- This setup is freely available: TradingView has many public "EMA 9 / VWAP crossover" scripts (one is in his favorites list).
- Could not verify: the membership's price or platform; the source of the P&L calendar.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment Replay" sends a DM with "the MCP + paper", which means the MCP URL (shown on screen: https://mcp.dasreplay.com/mcp) plus a DAS Replay sign-up. The account is the vendor itself, so this is direct advertising for a paid subscription: $39–79/mo after a 7-day card-on-file trial, or $999 lifetime. The "hedge fund strategy" is a hook.

### Verification & insights
- Fetched dasreplay.com, which confirmed the product, the company (Shark Belly Industries, LLC), pricing and data coverage. MCP isn't advertised on the homepage. Web search didn't index the Malhotra paper; its details come from the on-screen cover page.
- The demo doesn't do what the voice-over says. The Claude output is a tool QA checklist ("All 11 production tools passed… the script was deleted") with test scripts prefixed "QA prod", and the code shown is a volume-spike indicator. No strategy from the paper is built or backtested on screen.
- "Hedge fund just published their strategy" is false framing. Profitable funds don't publish live alpha; this is a 2018 consultant's "guidance" paper.
- A strategy backtested on a replay or simulated fill engine is still subject to overfitting, fill-model optimism, and costs and slippage. "10,000%" is a too-good-to-be-true number with zero evidence.
- Adding a third-party MCP connector gives that server access to your Claude session's tool calls. Claude's own dialog warns that Anthropic can't verify it.
- Could not verify: a public listing of the paper; what the DM contains beyond the MCP link.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
This is the vendor's own account advertising its product. "Comment REPLAY" → automated DM, almost certainly a link to the DAS Replay sign-up / 7-day trial (app.dasreplay.com/signup?plan=all_access…; card required), with paid plans $39–79/mo or $999 lifetime. ### Verification & insights
- dasreplay.com (home, pricing info, /changelog, /roadmap, /support help centre with ~60 articles) checked on 2026-10-21: no MCP server, no Claude integration and no AI features documented; Pine Script editor = "Planned". The reel's UI scenes are stylised animations, not screen recordings, so they are not evidence the features exist. Possibly a private beta, but unverified.
- Claims vs reality:
  - "Backtest your strategy in a forward-testing environment" = manual bar-by-bar replay (paper trading on historical tape) – useful practice, but not a statistical backtest; no win rate, P&L or drawdown is shown.
  - The demo "momentum strategy" is a textbook 9/20 EMA crossover indicator on one hand-picked +100% small-cap day – hindsight selection.
  - "Claude just turned every trader into a quant" – the presenter himself hedges ("at least that's what everyone's saying").
- If you want Claude + charting via MCP today, open-source TradingView MCP projects exist (e.g. tradesdontlie/tradingview-mcp, which drives TradingView Desktop – unofficial), independent of this vendor.
- Not verified: the rest of the caption, whether an MCP endpoint exists privately, the identity of the presenter.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Two layers. (1) **Engagement bait:** the on-screen card and caption tell viewers to comment which LuxAlgo indicator they use, promising a custom backtest ("Comment karo — kaun sa LuxAlgo indicator use karte ho?"). This farms comments/follows. (2) **Traffic to his own channels:** the reel ends on his profile and his YouTube "Unfiltered Finance With Sahil." The destination is his free finance content and audience growth ("follow me and message me"), not a priced product shown in the post. No affiliate/referral code to LuxAlgo is visible, so the LuxAlgo promotion appears unpaid (free promo). LuxAlgo itself is a paid indicator vendor.

### Verification & insights
- **The metrics table does not reconcile — a strong fabrication tell.** With win rate 55% (34/62), avg winner +2.12R and avg loser -1.00R, expected value per trade ≈ 0.55×2.12 − 0.45×1.00 ≈ **+0.72R**, not the stated "+0.09R". Profit Factor should be (34×2.12)/(28×1.00) ≈ **2.57**, not the stated 2.04. These figures were almost certainly generated for presentation rather than computed from a real trade log; the "Institutional Performance Metrics / appendix" styling is cosmetic.
- **"LuxAlgo's strategy" is a misnomer.** LuxAlgo sells closed indicators, not one published strategy. A 3-indicator Python re-implementation (two of the three are free LuxAlgo scripts; RSI is generic) is the creator's own approximation, not a LuxAlgo product or an officially endorsed strategy.
- **Single-year, single-index result is cherry-pick territory.** No out-of-sample period, no benchmark (Nifty 50's own return that year), no brokerage/STT/slippage, and swing entries on daily bars with only 62 trades in a year make 47.2% easy to overfit. The on-screen "Backtest only / past performance…" disclaimer is present but buried.
- **LuxAlgo claims (web-checked):** the card faithfully reproduces LuxAlgo's marketing (luxalgo.com, "world's largest provider," 1M+ TradingView followers, Pine Wizard 2024, Discord Partner, 150,000+ community). These are LuxAlgo's self-reported numbers, broadly consistent with its public TradingView profile, not independent rankings.
- **Creator credentials** (from his own on-screen cards): "8+ yrs Algo Trader, Ex-HFT, NISM-certified Research Analyst, Ex-Canara Bank, AMFI MF distributor." Not independently verified beyond his own pages.
- **Not executed / unverified:** the Python code and the backtest were not run; the trade list, data window, and costs are not shown, so none of the headline numbers could be confirmed.

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9:30 a.m. | time rule | audio 0.2s | The New York stock/index-futures cash open (9:30 ET). The "wake up at 9:30" framing is lifestyle copy. |
| London / Asia session high and low | levels | audio 5.6s | ICT (Inner Circle Trader) concept: the high and low of the Asian session (roughly 7–8 pm to midnight NY) and the London session (roughly 2–5 am NY), used as "liquidity" levels. Several free TradingView indicators draw them. |
| "Sweep" | concept | audio 5.6s | ICT term for price briefly trading through a prior high or low (a "liquidity grab") and reversing. |
| 1-minute time frame | parameter | audio 9.8s | Entry chart |
| IFVG | setup | audio 9.8s | Inversion fair value gap: a 3-candle imbalance ("FVG") that price closes through, after which it is treated as support or resistance in the opposite direction (ICT/SMC jargon). |
| 1:2R | risk:reward | audio 9.8s | Target = 2× the risk (stop distance) |
| Lamborghini Huracán | prop | page 1 | Used as proof of success. No ownership or trading results are shown. |
| #daytrader #trading | hashtags | caption | n/a |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No explicit call to action, link or product in the reel or the caption. The handle "tempoict" says it is an ICT-method account. These usually grow followers and later sell a mentorship, Discord or prop-firm funded-account referrals, but nothing of that kind is visible here, and a web search found no public profile or site for "tempoict".

### Verification & insights
- The steps are real ICT vocabulary and form a recognisable "session-liquidity sweep → 1-minute IFVG" model. But they are under-specified: they don't say which instrument (likely NQ/ES futures), which direction after the sweep, where the stop goes, what invalidates the trade, how many trades a day, or the time window.
- A 1:2R target is only profitable if the win rate stays above about 33.3% after costs. No win rate, sample or P&L is shown.
- ICT/"smart money concepts" have no peer-reviewed evidence of an edge. Results posted by creators are self-reported and survivorship-biased.
- "Lambo + it's that simple" is a classic lifestyle-marketing frame. Most retail day traders lose money (broker disclosures and academic studies of retail day trading, e.g. Barber, Lee, Liu and Odean on Taiwanese day traders, find the large majority unprofitable).
- Could not verify: who owns the car, the creator's identity, or any trading track record. A web search returned no information on "tempoict".

Contents: "20 strategies" for NinjaTrader 8, MT5 and TradingView; "QuantDash Pro" prop-firm journal and simulator; NT8 AI Agent Bridge; MotherEA MQL5 EA; Claude Code setup guide; private GitHub source access; VIP Discord. "All sales final", no refunds. |
| Seller claims | numbers | Whop listing (not in the reel) | "$250K+ in verified prop firm payouts", "41% CAGR, 14.87% max drawdown with private investors", "FTMO Platinum certified". All self-reported and unverified. The listing shows about 4.7★ from 17 reviews. |
| 1h and 4h time frames | parameters | audio 29.5s | The claimed new home of the classic strategies. |
| #algotrading #propfirm #quant #trading | hashtags | caption | n/a |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Premium for coders: link in bio" and "my private community" lead to ZenomTrader Premium on Whop: $150/month or $1,000/year, with a free trial, and no refunds once access is granted.
- The reel's on-screen checkout card names the product directly.
- Its prop-firm focus fits the #propfirm hashtag: the pitch is strategies for passing funded-account challenges.
- The handle (dennisg4368 / DenisG.) wasn't confirmed as the Whop owner from the listing, which names no individual. The link is inferred from the reel showing the product and "my private community".

### Verification & insights
- **What was checked:** the Carver book and author through the publisher; ZenomTrader Premium through its Whop listings and a web search.
- **Claim: trend following is robust and a top performer in Carver's book.** BROADLY TRUE as a long-run statement. Trend following on diversified futures has a long, documented track record. It also has long flat or losing stretches (the 2010s, for example).
- **Claim: 1980s strategies are "working better" on 1h/4h charts.** UNSUPPORTED and likely backwards.
  - No backtest, data, costs or out-of-sample results are shown.
  - Carver's own published work (his blog and later books) generally finds the fastest trend rules weaker after trading costs. Very short horizons tend to show mean reversion and noise rather than trend.
  - This counterpoint comes from general knowledge of Carver's writing and was not re-verified in this session.
- **Claim: signal accuracy rises because you "sample the same behaviour more often".** FLAWED. More bars give more trades, not a higher hit rate. Costs and slippage grow with trade frequency, and intraday bars are highly autocorrelated.
- **Claim: he coded the edge before reading the books.** Unverifiable, and it works as authority-building for the paid community.
- **Risk flags:**
  - The smooth equity curve on the checkout card is marketing with no audit.
  - Prop-firm payouts are not the same as verified, repeatable returns.
  - $150/month with no refunds is a strong commitment.
- **Free alternatives:** Carver's blog (qoppac.blogspot.com) and his open-source pysystemtrade cover the same "classic" trend rules.
- **Could not verify:** any performance figure, the identity link between @dennisg4368 and the Whop seller, and the illegible stats on the checkout card.

## Dem6pGCjhcs | @market.moves.matt | 9-step "flywheel" for selling 30-day cash-secured puts on leveraged ETFs after a 5% red day, closing at 50% profit
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/Dem6pGCjhcs/
- **Account:** @market.moves.matt · **Posted:** unknown (the mirror gave no date and yt-dlp hit HTTP 429) · **Type:** carousel (11 slides)
- **Caption source:** possibly truncated (mirror). I made one yt-dlp attempt and it returned HTTP 429 Too Many Requests, so I did not retry.
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** MISLEADING – the mechanics described are real, but "works 10X better than day trading" has no support. Selling about 5%-per-month puts on leveraged ETFs carries large, under-stated crash and assignment risk. The final step is held back to collect comments for a DM funnel.

### Post description
> Most traders think making more money means taking more trades
>
> But this system is built around waiting for ONE specific setup and repeating the same process every time it appears
>
> Comment FLYWHEEL and I’ll send you the full put-selling system step by...

(Cut off by the mirror. The rest is not retrievable.)

### Audio
None. This is an image carousel.

### On-screen content, page by page
Each slide is a lifestyle photo of the creator (restaurant terrace, sailboat, desert, mountains, resort pool, hot tub, coffee) with white text boxes.
**Slide 1:** "TRADER WHO GOT RICH SELLING OPTIONS SHARES A PUT-SELLING SYSTEM THAT WORKS 10X BETTER THAN DAY TRADING (swipe left)". The creator wears a **barchart** cap, and an inset circle shows a blurred options or quote board.
**Slide 2:** "Step 1: Pick 2-3 quality leveraged ETFs" – "Build a small watchlist of leveraged ETFs you understand and would actually be comfortable trading" / "Stick to those same 2-3 tickers instead of constantly searching for the next stock to trade"
**Slide 3:** "Step 2: Check the EMA clouds before entering" – "Look at the EMA clouds on each ticker and wait until both are showing green" / "If they are not both green, stay in cash and wait instead of forcing a trade"
**Slide 4:** "Step 3: Wait for a 5% red day" – "Once the trend checks out, wait for the ETF to pull back roughly 5% before doing anything" / "That red day can give you a better entry while increasing the premium available on the puts"
**Slide 5:** "Step 4: Open the options chain and go 30 days out" – "Look at put contracts expiring roughly 30 days from the day you enter" / "This gives the position time to work without locking your capital up for several months"
**Slide 6:** "Step 5: Find a strike paying roughly 5%" – "Compare the premium you collect with the cash required to secure the put" / "If you cannot find a strike offering the return you want at a price you're comfortable owning, skip the trade"
**Slide 7:** "Step 6: Sell the cash-secured put" – "Sell the put at your chosen strike and collect the premium upfront" / "If you eventually get assigned, you're buying an ETF you already chose at the strike price you agreed to"
**Slide 8:** "Step 7: Set a GTC order to close at 50% profit" – "Immediately place an order to buy the contract back once you've captured half of the available premium" / "Once it fills, your profit is locked in and the capital securing the position is freed up again"
**Slide 9:** "Step 8: Live your life until the next red day" – "Stop staring at the position and looking for reasons to interfere with your plan" / "Wait until another setup meets your rules before putting that capital back to work". Watermark: "MARKETMOVES".
**Slide 10:** "Before I reveal the final step..." / "Comment FLYWHEEL and I'll send you my free guide explaining this entire put-selling system step by step"
**Slide 11:** "Step 9: Repeat the system when the next setup appears" – "Wait for another 5% red day, sell another 30-day put, and set your GTC order to close at 50% profit" / "Once that trade closes, use the freed-up capital for the next qualifying setup and keep repeating the same process"

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Leveraged ETFs (2–3 tickers) | instrument | Slide 2 | No tickers are named. These are typically 2x or 3x products such as TQQQ, SOXL or UPRO (my examples, not from the post). |
| EMA clouds ("both green") | indicator | Slide 3 | Most likely the popular "Ripster EMA clouds" (paired EMA bands, for example 5/12 and 34/50). "Both green" means the short and long clouds are both bullish. The periods are not stated in the post. |
| 5% red day | rule | Slides 4, 11 | Entry trigger: the ETF falls about 5% in one day. |
| ~30 DTE puts | parameter | Slides 5, 11 | Standard premium-selling expiry. |
| Strike paying ~5% | parameter | Slide 6 | Premium ÷ cash secured ≈ 5% over 30 days, which is about 60% a year if annualised. Achievable only on very high-volatility underlyings, at strikes close to the money. |
| Cash-secured put | strategy | Slide 7 | Real, broker-supported strategy. |
| GTC buy-to-close at 50% profit | rule | Slides 8, 11 | Common "tastytrade-style" management rule. |
| "FLYWHEEL" | comment keyword | Caption, slide 10 | Auto-DM trigger for a "free guide". |
| barchart | brand on cap | Slide 1 | Barchart.com, a market-data site. No sponsorship is evident. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment FLYWHEEL" triggers an auto-DM with a "free guide". The comments boost the post's reach, and the guide is a lead magnet that usually asks for an email and leads to a paid community, course or newsletter. All 9 steps are already in the carousel: step 9, the "final step" held back on slide 10, is just "repeat". No price was visible. I found no web footprint for the account. The "got rich" framing over luxury-travel photos is standard guru marketing.

### Verification & insights
- The rules are internally coherent and use real mechanics. Nothing in them is false.
- "10X better than day trading" has no evidence: no backtest, track record or P&L is shown.
- Risks the post leaves out:
  - Leveraged ETFs can fall 30–80% within weeks (for example 3x Nasdaq and semiconductor ETFs in 2022 and 2020). A 5% red day is often the start of a larger drop, not the bottom.
  - A put paying about 5% in 30 days is priced for exactly that crash risk. Assignment means holding a decaying leveraged product, and volatility decay hurts long holding periods.
  - Short puts have small capped gains and large losses, so a few blow-ups can erase many 50%-profit wins.
  - Taxes and commissions are not mentioned.
- "Live your life" with no stop-loss or exit rule for losing positions is the critical gap. The system only defines the take-profit.
- Not verified: the creator's results, the posting date, and the content of the guide (I did not engage with the funnel).

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gamma Exposure University | product (coaching group) | Caption, audio 22s, page 6 | The creator's own application-gated programme ("work directly with me", 20 seats). Web search found nothing about it, and no price is shown. |
| GEX (gamma exposure) levels | concept | Throughout | Dealer gamma aggregated by strike from options open interest. It is used as support and resistance or "magnet" levels. Real concept, widely sold. |
| 5-point checklist | rules | Page 5, audio 15s | Trend; GEX levels; gamma environment (positive or negative gamma regime); compression at the level; volume expansion on the break. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment JOIN" triggers an auto-DM linking to the application form shown on page 6 (about 90 seconds to fill in). An application gate is the usual setup for a high-ticket coaching sale: you apply, get a sales call, then are offered a paid group or mentorship. "Work directly with me" points to coaching, which typically costs hundreds to thousands of dollars, though no price is shown. The scarcity is very likely artificial: the caption and audio disagree on seats left, and "for the first time ever" plus "close it tomorrow" are stock urgency lines. A web search found no public footprint for the programme or the creator.

### Verification & insights
- The checklist is generic GEX-trading lore, and the post demonstrates only one cherry-picked chart with no ticker, date or outcome. There is no backtest, win rate or track record.
- GEX levels are estimates. They depend on assumptions about whether dealers are long or short each option, and they shift intraday. Even GEX indicator authors warn against using them alone for entries and stops (a TradingView GEX script description says so).
- Free alternatives for learning GEX exist, for example "Gamma Exposure for Beginners" (Options Millionaire) recommended in Dej7wZLv-Ww, and SpotGamma's free educational posts.
- Not verified: the programme's price, content and the creator's results (I did not engage with the funnel).

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- Comment "WAITLIST" → an auto-DM link → **gammaexposureuniversity.com/waitlist** (a 45-second form) → a mandatory waitlist **Telegram channel** → the sales pitch. This was established in the reviews of the same campaign (DeAO0bpAUM7, DeKLxtVombQ, DeKgYDSgg8t).
- The operator is **NickelNinjaTradesLLC** (Colchester, VT). It is a 20-seat "work directly with me" mentorship with an "early-bird discount" and **no price shown**. It promises "Profitable in 90 days, or I'll keep working with you for free until you are", and shows unverified student P&L screenshots. Its own disclaimer says results are "not typical" and "most traders lose money".
- **ITMatrix** is ranked top again (he called it "worth every penny" in DeAO0bpAUM7). No affiliate code or "paid partnership" label is visible, and ITMatrix shows no public affiliate program, so a commercial tie is possible but unconfirmed. His company email domain, "ninjagex.com", shows he brands himself around GEX.

### Verification & insights
- **Fair point:** a price/volume indicator cannot measure dealer gamma. Real GEX needs open interest by strike and expiry plus an assumption about which side dealers hold. Note, though, that *every* vendor's GEX is a **model estimate**: none of them see actual dealer positions, and all infer them from open interest and assumptions. That includes ITMatrix and SpotGamma.
- **Ranking criteria:** the top two tiers are separated mainly by workspace features ("synchronized widgets", "session replay", "3D surfaces"), not by evidence of better predictive accuracy. No data is shown on whether any tool's levels improve trading results.
- **Free alternatives** for the concept: SqueezeMetrics' free "Gamma Exposure (GEX)" white paper, SpotGamma's free educational pages, the free ITMatrix tier, and open-source GEX calculators.
- **Not verified:** SpotGamma's and Tradytics' current prices, and the mentorship price (not published).
- Sources: [TradingView Gamma Hedging Pressure script](https://www.tradingview.com/script/G80P1pIX-Gamma-Hedging-Pressure), [TradingView "PRO" listing](https://my.tradingview.com/scripts/options-strategy/), [MenthorQ on ATAS marketplace](https://origin-marketplace.atas.net/product/menthor-q-market-data-services), [FlashAlpha SpotGamma review 2026](https://flashalpha.com/articles/spotgamma-review-2026-pricing-features-alternatives), [Tradytics on G2](https://www.g2.com/products/tradytics/pricing).

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Comment TRADE" → DM with the Astral (heyastral.ai) MCP link → you must pay $9 for month one, then $30/month, before any access. The same creator previously pushed Astral with a "comment ALGO" CTA (entry DedNh0GSXc3), and another creator ran the same Astral pitch (Ddei3-cy1cC). This points to a sponsorship or referral campaign. No disclosure appears in the caption or video.

### Verification & insights
- **Checked:** heyastral.ai home and pricing pages (MCP feature, broker list, $9→$30, no free trial). Every table row above was read by eye from the frames.
- **Claims vs reality:**
  - "+96%" (hook) vs "+91%" (table) vs "91% return with a 60% win rate" (audio; the table says 58%).
  - Picking the best of 11 variants after the fact is classic **overfitting / data snooping**. With 19 trades, the confidence interval on any statistic is enormous. Neither the sweep nor the earlier bot shows costs, slippage or an out-of-sample/walk-forward test.
  - The only fully visible backtest (RSI-2 on SPY) returned +1.7%/yr against SPY buy-and-hold's far higher return over 2015–2026. The sweep's +91% over an unknown period cannot be compared with QQQ buy-and-hold, which rose several hundred percent over 2015–2026.
  - "Just like that, Claude is trading for us": only the typed prompt "deploy the best strategy live" is shown. There is no confirmation of a broker connection, an order or a live position.
  - "Day trade" is a misnomer. EMA 20/50 regime trades (about 19 trades over years) are swing or position trades.
- Letting an LLM deploy live strategies through an MCP connector that has broker access is a real-money risk. Paper-trade first.
- **Not verified:** the sweep's date range and instrument; whether Lucas receives referral payments.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- The reel drives traffic to strongstocktrading.com, where the funnels are the free email brief, paid member courses, the store, the book, and the Alpha Engine waitlist. No prices were shown on the homepage. The ElevenLabs link in the caption is an affiliate link that earns the account a commission.

### Verification & insights
- **Claim "check out the live results"** is contradicted by the company's own site: no live dashboard and no published performance. The book blurb mentions a "33% live win rate during Alpha testing".
- **Claim "This isn't back-testing, it's forward-looking risk analysis"** is overstated. Monte Carlo on "randomized synthetic trade sequences" is normally built from the strategy's historical (backtested) trade distribution. It reshuffles past results rather than forecasting, and it inherits any bias in the backtest.
- **Claim "thousands of simulations every day... powering every trade SST makes"** is unverified, and the site says it makes "no claim of autonomous live trading".
- The reel is AI-generated end to end (script, persona and voice). Nothing in it can be checked, and it shows no numbers.
- Could not verify: any performance at all, or whether "Raul" corresponds to any real employee.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Commenting "access" gets a DM that adds you to a "waitlist", almost certainly for paid access to the invite-only TradingView bot and the "BOT Premium" Discord shown on screen. The usual setup: subscription access to a protected script, plus automation via a webhook bridge (e.g. PickMyTrade/TradersPost-style services) into prop-firm accounts. Discord payout screenshots are the social proof. No price is shown and none was found on the web. The bot name and the account turned up nothing in searches.

### Verification & insights
- **Backtest weakness:**
  - 82 trades over ~5.5 months is a small sample, shown in-sample (on the period the creator chose).
  - Commission and slippage settings are not shown.
  - "Default detalization" (no bar magnifier) on 15-min bars can misjudge intrabar stop/target fills.
  - Because the script is protected, repainting or look-ahead cannot be ruled out.
  - The +$12,638 / 82 trades ≈ $154 per trade. On MNQ ($2/point) that is ~77 points per trade at 1 contract, unusually large for a "range" scalp. Position size is unknown, so this is a plausibility flag, not proof.
- "93% chance of passing a 100K eval" has no basis on screen. It may come from a Monte-Carlo the viewer never sees.
- **Prop-firm rules:** "stack them" means running the bot on many funded accounts at once. Many prop firms restrict fully automated trading, copy-trading across accounts, or "group trading". Violations void payouts, so check each firm's current rules.
- The creator's own trading results are not shown, only a backtest and other members' payout posts.
- **Privacy:** the video exposes a personal Gmail address in a browser tab and private Discord usernames. They are not reproduced here.
- Could not verify: the bot's existence outside the Discord, its price, live performance, and the 93% figure.

- A web search found Whop pages "trademello-premium" and "free-tradmello" branded FootClanTrading, which describe order flow, volume footprint candles and the "Whale Pivot Model". No independent performance record was found.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Comment DESK" leads to an automated DM with six prompts plus a setup guide. It is a lead magnet; the account's "Work with me" Linktree is the paid end of the funnel. No price is shown in this post.
- **This carousel recycles the hook from the creator's own earlier video** (reviewed as Dropbox V24): "6-agent AI trading desk turned $10 into $4k overnight", also "comment DESK". That video's dashboard showed a $45,000 starting balance, memecoin tickers, a Spanish UI with "OWNER GUS", and different agent roles (e.g., ATLAS "watches the big wallets"). This carousel quietly reframes the result as "self-reported" and switches to paper trading.

### Verification & insights
- **Nothing technical to verify.** There is no repo, platform, broker, code or trade log. The images are AI-generated pixel art, and inconsistencies (odd axes, generic headlines such as "CHIP DEMAND FORECAST RISES") show the dashboards are not real screens.
- **Claims vs reality:**
  - "+$4,955 overnight": explicitly "self-reported" and not the creator's. Even taken at face value, 46 trades in one night with no position size, capital base or fees disclosed says nothing about expectancy. One good night is classic cherry-picking.
  - "6 AI agents ran a 24/7 trading desk": the creator's own version is paper trading only.
  - The role split and the "risk can veto" and "no consensus, no trade" rules are sensible design ideas, already implemented free in open-source projects. **TauricResearch/TradingAgents** (Apache-2.0, HEAD e8f5503 on 2026-10-16, v0.7.0) has fundamentals, sentiment, news and technical analysts, bull/bear researchers, a trader, a risk team and a portfolio manager.
- **Related posts:** DeYzh2Lm3-V (same creator, a Bull/Bear/Risk debate carousel, "comment DEBATE") and V24 (the original "$10 into $4k" video).
- **Could not verify:** whose result the "+$4,955" is, and the contents of the DM prompts and guide.
