## DepblAfgEqJ | @nickelninjatrades | Talking-head pitch for a GEX morning routine (ITMatrixHQ gamma map + EMA stack/flag/volume) that sells "Gamma Exposure University" with scarcity
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DepblAfgEqJ/
- **Account:** @nickelninjatrades (Nick Ireland) · **Posted:** 2026-10-18 · **Type:** reel (76.6s, 7 content pages + a black end frame)
- **Caption source:** full (Instagram)
- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** MISLEADING. GEX levels are a real concept and ITMatrixHQ is a real paid tool, but the "almost zero competition" and "entire system" claims have no evidence behind them. The caption's scarcity ("20 spots… 1 spot left… close it tomorrow") is a sales tactic for an unpriced course.

### Post description
> Comment “JOIN” to join Gamma Exposure University before I close it tomorrow.
>
> 20 spots only.
>
> 1 spot left.

### Audio
The creator on camera (English). Timestamped notes, with key lines quoted:
- [0.0] Hook: claims a trader who knows gamma exposure, zero DTE, open interest data, convexity exposure and the Delta Gamma Taylor expansion has "almost a zero competition in this market."
- [12.1] "But if you think that price action and fair value gaps run this market, you're totally cooked."
- [16.6] Morning routine: before market open he pulls up a gamma map, using **itmatrixhq.com**.
- [23.4] The largest levels, he says, show where dealers are forced to buy and sell to hedge; he treats them as the day's support, resistance and "magnet levels".
- [33.3] Claims the levels come from actual options positioning data, not lines someone drew on a chart.
- [38.5] Next, check the gamma environment: in positive gamma, price gets pinned to the largest levels; in negative gamma, it accelerates through them.
- [45.7] Then three confirmations: a clean EMA stack, price flagging at your level, and volume confirming the move.
- [52.6] "If all the confirmations line up, take the trade. If even one is missing, walk away from the screen."
- [58.8] Says you are not predicting, just reading the map and filtering whether the setup qualifies: "That's the entire system."
- [67.4] Call to action: "For the first time ever", he is opening Gamma Exposure University for 20 people who want to learn how he trades and work directly with him; comment "join" to save your spot.

### On-screen content, page by page
A split screen: the creator on top, with stylised serif captions.
**Page 1 (0–12.1s):** "MOST TRADERS HAVE NEVER STUDIED THIS". A collage of screenshots: a GEX profile chart (green and red bars by strike, x-axis "GEX"); a "THETA DECAY" chart ("Option Value" vs "Time Remaining Until Expiration Date", 120 → 0 days); "Option Volume and Open Interest (50 DTE)" bars by strike (about 185–220); a "Convexity Exposure — Broader term for positive gamma's curvature advantage" panel; and a "Delta-Gamma Taylor Expansion: The Quadratic Math Behind Gamma Curves" sheet.
**Page 2 (12.1–18.8s):** "BUT IF YOU THINK" over a whiteboard reading "Price Action" and "FVGs".
**Page 3 (18.8–33.3s):** "BEFORE THE …". A purple and teal GEX heat-map table (strike rows; values such as 48,931 / 1.79M; 163,666 / 1.32M; a yellow-highlighted **13.20M**; 3.89M; −199,892 / 2.36M; 973,122 / 858,948; −196,416 / −944,882; −52,617 / −30,765) with the overlay **"ITMatrixHQ.com"**. The strike/ticker column is cut off.
**Page 4 (33.3–38.5s):** "THESE LEVELS". The same table, with labels "Options Positioning Data" vs "Random Lines".
**Page 5 (38.5–45.8s):** "THEN CHECK". The same heat map.
**Page 6 (45.8–58.9s):** "FROM THERE". A TradingView-style chart with the text "Target", "- EMA Stack", "- Flag", "- Volume Confirmation", plus chart labels "Break", "Bull Flag Pattern" and "Volume Expansion".
**Page 7 (58.9–74.8s):** "YOU'RE NOT TRYING" over a whiteboard reading "Predicting".
**Page 8 (74.8–76.6s):** Black end frame.

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| ITMatrixHQ.com | site/tool | audio 20.9s, page 3 | Verified: an options market-structure terminal (GEX Matrix, 3D surface, flow, 0DTE, API). Pricing: **Free** (one daily 9am CT snapshot, ~10 symbols, 15-min delay), **Charts $29.99/mo**, **Basic $69.99/mo** (7-day trial via Whop), **Pro $119.99/mo**, **API $119.99/mo**; 15% off annual. The site names no owner and doesn't mention Nick Ireland, so any affiliation is unknown. |
| Gamma exposure, 0DTE, open interest, convexity exposure, delta-gamma Taylor expansion | concepts | audio 2.4–9.6s, page 1 | Standard options terms. The Taylor expansion (ΔP ≈ Δ·dS + ½Γ·dS²) is basic options math, not a secret edge. |
| Price action, FVGs (fair value gaps) | concepts | audio, page 2 | ICT/SMC retail concepts that the video dismisses. |
| EMA stack, flag, volume confirmation | entry filters | audio 45.7s, page 6 | Generic technical-analysis confirmations. No EMA periods are given. |
| "Gamma Exposure University" | course | caption, audio 67.4s | The creator's own course/mentorship. Web search found no public page or price. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment JOIN" gets a DM leading to **Gamma Exposure University**, a paid cohort or mentorship ("work directly with me"). It uses classic scarcity: "20 spots only", "1 spot left", "close it tomorrow", "for the first time ever". The price is not shown. ITMatrixHQ is also prominently plugged (possibly an affiliate or partner; unverified). A viewer would need an ITMatrixHQ subscription ($29.99–$119.99/mo) to follow the routine.

### Verification & insights
- The GEX mechanics stated (positive gamma pins, negative gamma accelerates) are the mainstream view, but GEX levels are model estimates built on assumptions about dealer positioning. Many retail vendors (SpotGamma, Menthor Q, GEXBot, ITMatrixHQ, etc.) sell them, so "almost zero competition" is false.
- The "system" (GEX level + positive/negative regime + EMA stack, flag, volume) comes with no win rate, backtest, timeframe, instrument, stop or target rules. It cannot be evaluated.
- Scarcity claims ("1 spot left") can't be verified and are a standard pressure tactic.
- Not verified: course price and content, Nick Ireland's track record, and whether he has a financial tie to ITMatrixHQ.

- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DenaTWExFIh/

- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** MISLEADING. It is self-labelled a hypothetical backtest. A 95.1% win rate with only a 2.08 profit factor implies tiny targets and large stops (the average loser is roughly 10× the average winner), so a few bad days can erase months. No live record, no rules disclosed, and the caption is cut before what is likely the pitch.

### Post description
> UltimateORB takes about two trades a day and is usually done by noon. Here are all 1,396 of its backtest trades as an equity curve (1 micro contract, after commissions): 95.1% winners, profit factor 2.08, 31 of 37 months profitable. Then two normal s…

(Cut off by the mirror. The remainder, probably the punchline and the CTA, was not retrievable.)

### Audio
Not available. The video was not retrievable (Instagram 429); this entry was reviewed from the cover image only.

### On-screen content, page by page
**Cover (only frame available):** Dark grid background. Top left: "■ QUANTSTACK"; top right: "NQ · 2023–2026". Headline: "ULTIMATEORB · BACKTEST" / "**1,396 TRADES**" (the number in teal). Sub-label: "BACKTEST · 1 MICRO CONTRACT · AFTER COMMISSIONS". An empty equity-curve axis from "OCT 2023" to "OCT 2026" with a starting dot (the curve presumably animates in the video). Footer disclaimer: "HYPOTHETICAL BACKTEST, NOT LIVE TRADING. PAST RESULTS DON'T GUARANTEE FUTURE RESULTS."
Video not retrievable – reviewed from cover image only.

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| UltimateORB | strategy/product | caption, cover | The creator's opening-range-breakout system. No public listing tied to QuantStack was found; unrelated products with similar names exist (a TradingView "ORB Ultimate" indicator, an MQL5 "Ultimate ORB" EA). |
| NQ (micro = MNQ) | ticker | cover | CME Micro E-mini Nasdaq-100 futures ($2 per index point) |
| 1,396 trades; ~2/day; done by noon | numbers | caption, cover | 37 months × ~21 days × ~2 ≈ 1,550, so consistent |
| 95.1% winners | number | caption | ≈1,328 winners / 68 losers |
| Profit factor 2.08 | number | caption | Gross profit / gross loss |
| 31 of 37 months profitable | number | caption | ≈84% of months |
| Oct 2023 – Oct 2026 | period | cover | Backtest window |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
The CTA was cut off, so it is unknown. The handle and branding ("quantstackai") suggest a seller of automated futures strategies or bots (typically a subscription or a strategy license, often marketed to prop-firm traders). A web search found no site or price for "QuantStack AI"/UltimateORB. Treat it as a product pitch until shown otherwise.

### Verification & insights
- **Arithmetic check** (by hand, nothing executed). With win rate w = 0.951 and PF = 2.08, PF = w·AvgWin / ((1−w)·AvgLoss), so AvgLoss / AvgWin = 0.951 / (0.049 × 2.08) ≈ **9.3**. Each loser wipes out about 9 average winners. That profile (small scalps with wide or no stops) is fragile. Slippage of a tick or two per trade on tiny targets, or one gap or news spike, can flip the result.
- 6 of 37 months losing despite a 95% hit rate confirms that losses cluster.
- "After commissions" does not mean after slippage; slippage isn't mentioned on the cover.
- **Backtest risks:** ORB parameters (range length, targets, filters) can easily be fitted to 2023–2026, a strongly trending bull period for NQ. Bar-based backtests of intraday TP/SL often mis-handle which level is hit first inside a bar, which inflates win rates.
- The disclaimer "HYPOTHETICAL BACKTEST, NOT LIVE TRADING" is accurate and important (CFTC Rule 4.41 style language).
- **Could not verify:** the video's equity curve; the rest of the caption; the strategy rules; the seller or price.

- **Verdict:** MISLEADING. The setup is a vague intermarket-divergence plus RSI idea with no defined parameters, demonstrated in TradingView *Replay* (hindsight). The claims that "non-traders" are "making really good money" are unevidenced, and the post exists to drive sign-ups.

### Audio
The creator speaks to camera. There is no identifiable music. Timestamped notes, with key lines quoted:

- [4.0–11.6] Says even a "complete idiot" or "complete dummy" in the stock market can put one blue line on the chart, which is gold.

- [16.6–23.5] Third line: a black line in the lower pane, the relative strength index. "That's all we need, these three lines."

- [35.1–38.6] Recap: "Blue line going up, orange line going down, black line touches the bottom."
- [38.6–55.5] Claims he has taught this to his friends, family and "thousands of people" besides; most are non-traders using it, many are monetizing it, and "some of them are making really good money with it."

- [60.9–69.2] Call to action: he can invite viewers to one of those calls. "So if you want that, just hit the link. I'll hook you up."

### On-screen content, page by page
The burned-in captions repeat the speech word for word. Only the distinct visuals are listed.
**Page 1 (0.0–4.0s):** Red title box "How is this not illegal?". Creator selfie in a dark-green office. Caption "I'M NOT SURE IF THIS IS LEGAL OR NOT,"
**Page 2 (4.0–~9s):** Caption "YOU CAN BE A COMPLETE IDIOT."
**Pages 3–11 (~9–22s):** Fast camera swing to the monitor (blurred), with captions "THIS ONE BLUE LINE IN THE CHART," / "AND YOU CAN PUT THIS AN ORANGE LINE," / "WHICH IS THE DOW JONES," / "AND THEN PUT THIS" / "DOWN HERE, WHICH IS THE" / "RELATIVE STRENGTH INDEX."

The overlay indicator is labelled **"20-Minute Trader Macro Trend · YM"** (YM = CBOT Mini Dow futures). Y-axis values run about **49,360–49,580**, on the Dow scale. A lower pane is labelled **"RSI"**.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- **Who:** Jeremy Russell, author of *How to Be a 20-Minute Trader* (Wiley, 2023/24; hardcover ~$28–30). He runs the "20-Minute Trader" brand, which has an online community/course (a Circle-hosted product listing exists) and a free introductory course with paid advanced tiers, according to a 2023 press release.
- **The CTA:** "Comment CLASS" sends a DM with a link to a free Zoom "class"/webinar. This is a classic webinar-to-upsell funnel. A third-party review lists the entry price as low as $7, then paid tiers. I could not confirm current official tier prices.
- **Signs of an upsell:** The indicator on screen is branded "20-Minute Trader Macro Trend". The real "edge" is a proprietary script you would get by joining.

### Verification & insights
- **The rule as stated:** (1) gold rising while the Dow falls (divergence), and (2) RSI "touches the bottom", then (3) "jump in". No instrument to trade is specified (gold? Dow? which direction?). No timeframe, RSI length, oversold threshold, stop, target or position size is given. As shown, it cannot be tested.
- **Hindsight:** The demo runs in TradingView **Replay** on a historical session (8 Jan 2026). Picking a replay where the pattern worked is cherry-picking.
- **"Non-traders making really good money", "thousands of people":** No evidence, account statements or track record are shown. These are testimonial-style income claims typical of trading-education funnels.
- **"How is this not illegal?":** This is clickbait. Intermarket divergence plus RSI is ordinary, legal technical analysis. Nothing about it is secret.
- **Costs and realism:** A 5-second gold chart implies scalping. Spreads, commissions and slippage would dominate any small edge, and none of this is mentioned.
- **Not verified:** the actual contents and price of the "CLASS" Zoom session; any performance statistics.
- Sources: [Wiley – How to Be a 20-Minute Trader](https://wiley-vch.de/en/areas-interest/finance-economics-law/how-to-be-a-20-minute-trader-978-1-394-20522-6), [Business Wire 2023 release](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20230307005200/en), [Circle listing](https://discover.circle.so/product/20-minute-trader), [Goodreads review blog](https://www.goodreads.com/author_blog_posts/24033860-20-minute-trader-review).

- **"Comment ALGO" sends the Astral link.** Astral is a closed, paid SaaS ($9 first month, then $30/month). The site shows no public affiliate program, but a creator DM-ing "the link to this platform" is a typical referral or sponsorship pattern. No disclosure appears in the caption.

- **Astral** verified via its site (heyastral.ai): features, broker list and pricing as above.
- **Claims vs reality:**
  - "+$7,500 on my bots": no live account, broker statement or trade list is shown. The backtest (+$8,395) is a different number and is not live P&L.
  - "Just like that, I built the trading bot": a 23-trade backtest over months is a tiny sample. A Sharpe of 0.96 and max drawdown of −9.08% are ordinary, and costs and slippage assumptions are not shown.
  - The ChatGPT idea is a short-selling pairs strategy, but the Astral result is labelled "Long-Only", so the bot is not the "exact strategy" pasted in. GOOG/GOOGL (two share classes of the same company) is a very thin spread trade where costs dominate.
- **Free alternative:** the same idea-to-code-to-backtest loop can be done with a local backtester (e.g., backtesting.py or vectorbt) and an LLM, at no subscription cost.
- **Could not verify:** the creator's bio, link and any sponsorship (Instagram 429), and any live trading results.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Comment trade" triggers a DM with a free guide, which is a lead magnet. Per a third-party creator-analytics profile, @tenfoldmarc (Marc Cleroux, ~100K IG followers) is a Claude Code educator who runs an off-platform **"AI Builders" community**; the guide most likely funnels there. No price was found.
- The repo, the MCP server and Claude Code setup are all free. The real costs are a TradingView paid plan and a Claude subscription.

### Verification & insights
- Clone of tradingview-mcp-jackson: README and LICENSE read, last commit 2026-05-11 ("add subscribe-prompt.cjs — animated figlet SUBSCRIBE? banner", a YouTube-promo touch). Not executed.
- **What it actually does:** Claude reads and controls *your local* TradingView chart (symbols, indicators, drawings) through a debug port. It does not trade, place orders, or generate profit by itself. "Draw levels on the SPX" is the demo.
- **The "$10,000/month" claim is unsupported clickbait.** No strategy, results or costs are shown.
- **Risk:** enabling a remote-debugging port on an app where you are logged in, and letting an LLM drive it, carries security risk. Automating TradingView this way may also conflict with TradingView's ToS, which the repo itself flags as the user's responsibility.
- **Not verified:** the guide's contents; "AI Builders" community pricing.
- Source: [CreatorDB – tenfoldmarc](https://creatordb.app/creatorstats/tenfoldmarc/).

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "CMNT 'Access'" puts you on a waitlist, delivered by auto-DM, for the bot.
- The Discord server's name ("LETS FN GO BOT **Premium**") and its #payouts channel show a subscription bot community whose members run the bot on prop-firm evaluations.
- No price appears in the post. Expect a recurring fee, possibly with prop-firm affiliate or discount links; neither was confirmed.

### Verification & insights
- **The Monte Carlo does not prove an edge.** Shuffling or resampling the same 138 backtested trades only re-orders the in-sample results. If the backtest is overfit, the simulation inherits that bias, and the 93% figure is circular.
  - The method assumes trades are independent and that future conditions match the past year.
  - The report reads as auto-generated marketing ("institutional capital", "allocators").
- **Drawdown contradiction:** The strategy tester shows a max drawdown of **$2,070**, above the **$2,000** maximum loss limit that the report says the 73% win rate keeps you away from. Some historical path would therefore have failed the eval. TradingView measures drawdown differently from end-of-day trailing, but this is still a red flag.
- **Backtest realism:** About one year of MNQ data and 138 trades is a small sample. Commissions and slippage are not shown, and a 73% win rate with the TP/SL structure shown says little about risk/reward.
- **Prop-firm economics:** Passing a simulated evaluation is not the same as profit. Fees run about $49–$150 a month, and the payouts shown are small: the staff "cash account" balance was $1,614.38. Many prop firms restrict third-party or shared bots and copy trading; check the specific firm's rules.
- **Payout screenshots:** these are cherry-picked survivors. The post shows nothing about members who failed.
- **Could not verify:** the bot's actual rules, its live performance, its price, or the account's other links. I did not search the Discord usernames.

There is no primary source, owner identity or performance data. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment GROK BOT if you want to know how to build it" is a comment-to-DM automation that grows engagement and leads. Nothing in the post shows a build, so whatever arrives in the DM (a guide, a template, a paid community or a signals product) is unknown. Web searches for "msbintel" found no product or site, and the account's bio link could not be checked. Grok Bot access itself requires a premium plan. The video looks like a generic, possibly repurposed talking-head edit with stock B-roll, typical of faceless "AI news" pages.

### Verification & insights
- Web searches confirmed Grok Bot's launch (Aug 2026, beta, paid tiers). The "350 bots" story appears only in social reposts and promo articles; no independent confirmation was found.
- Realism check:
  - 350 agents passing messages 24/7 means large API and compute cost, which is ignored.
  - There is no evidence of profitability, drawdowns or the trading venue.
  - "Barely touches the system" plus "only trades that need approval" is self-contradictory marketing.
  - LLM agents reading news and on-chain data are prone to hallucination and prompt injection, so autonomous trading carries real financial risk.
- Survivorship and hype signals: an anonymous "someone", "changed trading forever", "so easy".
- Related posts: the same Grok Bot launch appears in @gennaroautomates DdUu9f_uBL0. A concrete, inspectable alternative for the TradingView-alert → AI-agent idea is in @arwazaz_ DdmWn-ME9wh (Hermes).

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- **Step 1:** comment "EXPECT" or join the Telegram channel to get a free MT5 EA (lead capture, 81-subscriber channel).
- **Step 2:** upsell to OriginFX Algo subscriptions at **$50 for 30 days / $149 for 90 days / $289 for 180 days**, paid through a Telegram bot. These give access to the "Origin Structured" gold EA and the "Super Trend Grid" scalper, running on a VPS the vendor provides. The broker sign-up links appear to be Vantage referral (affiliate) links, so the vendor may also earn from broker commissions.

### Verification & insights
- **Internal contradiction:** the reel's own expectancy panel gives +$2,180 per 100 trades ($21.80 × 100). The "ACCOUNT GREW" panel shows +$26,940 (+26.9%) for the same 100 trades, about 12 times more. Both cannot be true. With $130 risk per trade on a $100K account (0.13% risk), +26.9% is impossible from these numbers.
- **"$50K → $1.2M in 10 months, VERIFIED / AUDITED":** this is a 24-fold gain. The vendor's own site shows only a backtest of $50K → $240K (Jan to Sep 2026) with a 25% drawdown. No myfxbook-style third-party verified track record was found. Treat the claim as false or unverifiable.
- **"1% to 2% every day... without worrying about losses":** compounding 1%/day over ~250 trading days is about 12× a year, and 2%/day is about 140×. These are classic too-good-to-be-true numbers. A grid "recovery" EA is a well-known blow-up risk: it adds positions against the trend and can wipe out the account. "Without worrying about losses" is a misleading risk statement, and it contradicts the site's own "you can lose some or all of your capital" disclaimer.
- **The free EA:** the Telegram file for this strategy is 1.5 KB, essentially a minimal script. Nothing was downloaded or executed. A 46%/1:1.54 edge of +0.17R before costs on EURUSD M30 is thin, and the backtest gives no commission, swap, out-of-sample or date-range information.
- No regulator, company address or licence appears anywhere. Selling automated trading systems with performance promises can fall under financial-promotion rules in many jurisdictions.
- Could not verify: who is behind Origin FX, or any live trading record.

- **Category:** Trading strategy
- **Verdict:** NO SUBSTANCE / MISLEADING. A buzzword list with no rules or data. The "zero competition" claim is false. The graphics are partly generic or AI-made: one footprint slide contradicts its own legend, and the "Delta" card is the Investopedia definition of options delta, not order-flow delta. Its purpose is to collect "PPS" comments for a DM guide.

### Post description
> Order flow doesn't predict the move — it explains it as it happens. Most traders react to price. Order flow traders read what's driving it.
>
> 💬 "PPS" for my complete trading guide

### Audio
The creator speaks to camera, overlaid on a collage. I transcribed it myself with faster-whisper small, 1 thread, and corrected it against the burned-in captions:
- [0.0–1.9] "If you're a trader and you know what any of these are,"
- [1.9–6.9] "absorption, exhaustion, delta, delta divergence, stop runs, order imbalances,"
- [6.9–8.4] "then you have zero competition."
- [8.4–12.8] "If you think that order blocks and fair value gaps move the market, you're cooked."

### On-screen content, page by page
Burned-in yellow/white captions repeat the speech ("IF YOU'RE A", "DELTA DELTA DIVERGENCE", "STOP RUNS", "ORDER IMBALANCES", "AND YOU HAVE", "ZERO COMPETITION", "IF YOU THINK THAT ORDER BLOCKS AND FAIR VALUE MOVE THE MARKET", "YOU'RE COOKED").

**Page 1 (0.0s, cover):** A collage of all the graphics below around the creator's face, with the caption "TRADER".

**Page 2 (~1.9s):** "**ABSORPTION**", a hand-annotated footprint chart (green/red bid×ask cells) with arrows to a highlighted cell at a low, plus the labels "BID" and "ASK" and a blue horizontal level.

**Page 3 (~2.6s): "EXHAUSTION ON FOOTPRINT"** infographic. "Exhaustion occurs when aggressive buying or selling hits the market, but there is not enough opposing liquidity to keep the move going. High volume, large delta, little to no follow through. This shows us the market is losing momentum and a reversal is likely." Callouts: "EXHAUSTION SELLING – Large red delta · High volume · Little to no follow through lower · Buy imbalance appears after", "CLIMAX / EXHAUSTION", "PRICE FAILS TO MAKE NEW LOWS", "BUY IMBALANCE AFTER EXHAUSTION". The DELTA row reads −4, 5, 12, 8, 15, −7, −11, −15, −28, −42, −148, −98, −79, −27, 13, 44, 78. The VOLUME row reads 620, 637, 688, 742, 801, 845, 950, 1.1K, 1.2K, 1.4K, 2.3K, 1.8K, 1.2K, 1.3K, 1.4K, 1.6K, 1.3K. Times run 09:30–11:00. "KEY TAKEAWAY – Exhaustion shows us that buyers or sellers are no longer committed. Watch for a shift in order flow and look for the opposite side to take control."

**Page 4 (~3.4s):** "**Delta** [ˈdel-tə] – An estimate of the change in price of a derivative caused by a $1 change in the price of its underlying security." (An Investopedia card, a short definition quoted from Investopedia.) Caption "DELTA DELTA DIVERGENCE".

**Page 5 (~4.4s): "DELTA DIVERGENCE"**: price makes a "New High" while "Cumulative Delta" makes a "Lower High".

**Page 6 (~5.2s): "STOP RUNS"**: a price line rising through a level marked "Stops Triggered" above "Market Price".

**Page 7 (~6.1s): "ORDER IMBALANCE ON FOOTPRINT"**: "An order imbalance occurs when there is a large difference between buy orders (at the ask) and sell orders (at the bid) within a price level." It is a footprint grid with prices 5123.00–5126.00 in 0.25 steps (S&P 500 e-mini–style) and columns 10:14:30, 10:15:00, 10:15:30, 10:16:00 and 10:16:30. The column DELTA row reads **+178, +202, +175, +132, +415**. The highlighted cell at **5124.75 shows 215 | 28**. Callout: "**BULLISH IMBALANCE** – At price 5124.75, there were 215 buy orders (at the ask) vs 28 sell orders (at the bid). This large imbalance shows aggressive buying lifting offers and taking control." Legend: "HOW TO READ FOOTPRINT – Each cell shows: **Left = Bid Volume (market sells)**, **Right = Ask Volume (market buys)**; example 48 | 32 (Sell orders hitting bids | Buy orders lifting offers)." Key takeaway: "Imbalances show aggressive participation from one side. They often lead to continual buying/selling as price moves." Footnote: "Imbalances are not a guarantee, but they reveal the intent of large participants in real time."

**Page 8 (~8–12.8s):** Back to the collage (absorption, exhaustion, delta divergence, delta card, stops-triggered, footprint, and volume-profile histograms) with the remaining captions.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Commenting "PPS" gets a DM with the creator's "complete trading guide". This is a lead magnet; the 131 comments against 618 likes show the keyword-gate at work. No price, broker or prop-firm link is visible in the post. Web search found no public page for "fibsandflow" or a "PPS" product, so what "PPS" stands for (perhaps a named setup or system) and any later paid offer (course, mentorship, signals) could not be confirmed. The usual pattern for such accounts is a free PDF followed by an upsell.

### Verification & insights
- The reel was re-fetched with yt-dlp (it succeeded on one attempt). I extracted 8 scene frames and 26 frames at 2 fps and transcribed the audio myself. The full caption came from Instagram.
- "Zero competition" is false. Footprint and order-flow tools (Sierra Chart, ATAS, Bookmap, NinjaTrader order flow) are widely sold and used, and knowing the terms gives no proven edge.
- The order-flow view ("explains the move as it happens" rather than predicting it) is a fair framing from the caption. But the post gives no testable method, and its own graphic gets bid/ask sides confused.
- Not verified: the contents of the "PPS" guide and any paid upsell.

### Post description
> Comment "WAITLIST" to get on the waitlist for GAMMA EXPOSURE UNIVERSITY before I launch it to the public on October 11th

### Audio
The creator speaks to camera with a studio mic. Word-by-word burned-in subtitles. Background music bed. Timestamped notes, with key lines quoted:
- [0.0–5.8] Hook: "There are only two types of traders that end up winning in this market." Everything else, he says, is unsustainable long term.
- [5.8–15.0] The trader on the left trades the dealer's constraint, not candlesticks; gamma exposure maps dealer convexity across the options surface. (Whisper wrote "confecity"; from context he says convexity.)
- [15.0–23.4] In positive gamma, dealer hedging is counter-cyclical: dealers sell rallies and buy dips, suppressing volatility and pinning price.
- [23.4–34.2] In negative gamma, hedging is pro-cyclical: dealers buy strength and sell weakness, amplifying volatility and extending moves. "It's not a forecast, it's the hedging function underneath price."
- [34.2–52.7] The trader on the right trades what the data says is statistical edge, not what the market looks like. Cites Jim Simons treating markets as probabilistic systems: thousands of signals, decades of data, relentlessly tested edges; if the data showed no positive expectancy after risks and costs, "the trade did not exist."
- [52.7–62.0] Best approach is in the middle: "Gamma defines the regime, statistics quantify the edge." Structure identifies the state; expectancy decides whether it deserves capital.
- [62.0–70.5] Call to action: he is opening **Gamma Exposure University** for **20 people** who want to become consistently profitable working directly with him. "Comment **waitlist** to save your spot."

### On-screen content, page by page
The top 40% is the creator on camera with subtitles. The bottom is a whiteboard canvas (Figma-style guides).
**Page 1 (0.0–3.4s):** Box "YOU NEED BOTH TO WIN". Columns "TRADER A" and "TRADER B" (the full layout is described under page 3).
**Page 2 (3.4–5.8s):** The words "IFVG", "ICT" and "Price Action" scattered on the canvas. A red hand-drawn slash strikes them out ("everything else is unsustainable").
**Page 3 (5.8–62.0s):** "YOU NEED BOTH TO WIN".

  1. A magenta/teal GEX heat-map grid (strike × expiry table, the ITMatrix-style GEX heatmap he promotes elsewhere).
  2. A 3-D magenta gamma surface.
  3. A horizontal bar chart of gamma by strike ("strike profile").
- **TRADER B:**
  1. A photo of Jim Simons in front of a blackboard of equations.
  2. A Bloomberg-terminal-style dual-monitor trading desk.
  3. A fan of Monte-Carlo simulated equity paths.

  The cursor selects images as he talks about each one. There is no other text.

For exclusive updates, **you MUST join the Telegram.**", "⏱ Takes 45 sec", green "Start" button.

### Everything named or linked

|---|---|---|---|

The waitlist is at gammaexposureuniversity.com/waitlist and the site is operated by NickelNinjaTradesLLC. Details are from the earlier review of his post DeAO0bpAUM7 (below). |

The description (long gamma means dealers hedge against the move and dampen volatility; short gamma means they hedge with the move and amplify it) is the standard account. It assumes dealers are net short or long in the way GEX models infer, which is an estimate. |
| Jim Simons | person | audio 39.9s, p3 photo | Founder of Renaissance Technologies (Medallion Fund). The description of the approach (many signals, decades of data, tested edges) is a fair generic summary. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- Comment "WAITLIST" → DM link → **gammaexposureuniversity.com/waitlist** → a 45-second form → a mandatory waitlist **Telegram channel**.
- From the review of the same creator's DeAO0bpAUM7 (same caption opening, same campaign): the site is run by **NickelNinjaTradesLLC** (nick@ninjagex.com, Colchester, VT). It sells a 20-person mentorship with an "early-bird discount" and **no price shown**. It promises "Profitable in 90 days, or I'll keep working with you for free until you are". It displays unverified student P&L screenshots. Its own earnings disclaimer says results are "not typical" and "most traders lose money". The waitlist closed Oct 9, ahead of the Oct 11 public launch.
- A near-identical sister reel with the same caption exists: DeKLxtVombQ (same day).
- He separately recommends the paid ITMatrix GEX heatmap ("worth every penny"), a possible undisclosed commercial tie (unconfirmed).

### Verification & insights
- **Content accuracy:** the gamma mechanics are correctly described, and "it's not a forecast" is a fair caveat. But the video gives no rule, level, threshold or example trade. It is purely conceptual.
- **"Only two types of traders win" / "become consistently profitable":** marketing claims with no evidence. Combining regime filters (GEX) with expectancy testing is reasonable advice in itself, but the promise of consistent profitability for mentees is unsupported, and his own site disclaims it.
- **Jim Simons framing:** borrowing Renaissance's reputation to sell a retail options mentorship is an authority-transfer device. Medallion's methods are proprietary and unrelated to this course.
- **Risk:** expect a high-ticket upsell via Telegram. Price unknown.
- **Not verified:** the mentorship price, any track record, and whether the Oct 11 launch happened as stated.

**Slide 1 (1/8):** Title "**I BUILT AN AI TRADING ROOM THAT ARGUES BEFORE EVERY TRADE.**" A round table with seats BULL, BEAR and RISK. Screens read "UPSIDE" (green) and "DOWNSIDE" (red), and a central console reads "DECISION: WAIT". Footer tagline: "**NO CLEAR WINNER. NO TRADE.**"
**Slide 2 (2/8):** "**MOST BOTS ONLY HEAR ONE SIDE.** That creates biased decisions." A blocked-off red panel "IGNORED WARNINGS" holds three warnings: "OVERBOUGHT", "BAD NEWS", "HIGH RISK". The bot's screen shows "BUY" with checkboxes "TREND ✓ MOMENTUM ✓ VOLUME ✓".
**Slide 3 (3/8):** "**THE BULL BUILDS THE CASE TO BUY.** Momentum. Growth. Catalysts. Upside." Four panels (MOMENTUM, GROWTH, CATALYST, UPSIDE) feed a "BUY CASE" checklist with the same four items ticked. Chair label "BULL AGENT".
**Slide 4 (4/8):** "**THE BEAR FINDS EVERY REASON TO WAIT.** Weakness. Valuation. Bad news. Downside." A falling candlestick chart (y-axis 70–120, no ticker) and panels WEAKNESS, VALUATION, BAD NEWS, DOWNSIDE feed a "BEAR CASE" checklist with all four ticked, ending in "WAIT". Chair label "BEAR AGENT".
**Slide 5 (5/8):** "**THEN THEY ARGUE POINT BY POINT.** Every claim needs evidence." A central "EVIDENCE CHECK" board compares bull and bear evidence on four rows: TREND, VOLUME, NEWS, VALUATION (VALUATION has a scales icon).
**Slide 6 (6/8):** "**RISK MAKES THE FINAL CALL.** Buy. Wait. Or reject." "BULL REPORT" (4 ticks) and "BEAR REPORT" (4 warnings) feed "BOTH REPORTS REVIEWED". The risk checklist has SIZE ✓, STOP ✓, EXPOSURE ✓, VOLATILITY ✓. Buttons BUY / WAIT / REJECT, with "CURRENT DECISION" on WAIT. Chair "RISK AGENT".
**Slide 7 (7/8):** "**NO CLEAR WINNER? NO TRADE.** UNCERTAINTY IS A REASON TO WAIT." Scoreboard: "BULL 72 | TIE | BEAR 72". The console shows "WAIT". Footer: "SIMULATED SYSTEM • NOT FINANCIAL ADVICE".
**Slide 8 (8/8):** "**WANT THE FULL AI DEBATE SYSTEM? COMMENT DEBATE**". Three cards: "BULL PROMPT", "BEAR PROMPT", "RISK PROMPT", then "+ PAPER-TRADING SETUP GUIDE". Footer: "SIMULATE FIRST • NOT FINANCIAL ADVICE".

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
- "Comment DEBATE" triggers an automated DM with three role prompts plus a setup guide. It is a lead magnet for follower and DM-list growth.
- The account's bio says "AI systems that make money … 📩 Work with me ↓" and links to a Linktree, so the end of the funnel is the creator's services or offers. No price or product appears in this post.
- No affiliate or broker link is visible.
- The same creator posted the "6 AI agents ran a 24/7 trading desk overnight" carousel (DeaQMKLgeT5), and an earlier "$10 into $4k overnight" desk video was reviewed as Dropbox V24. Both use the same comment-for-prompts template.

### Verification & insights
- **There is nothing to verify technically.** No code, repo, backtest, ticker or performance figure is shown. The slides are AI-generated illustrations and the charts are decorative.
- **The idea is real and already free.** A bull/bear debate judged by a risk manager is the core design of **TauricResearch/TradingAgents** (verified with `git ls-remote`; HEAD e8f5503, 2026-10-16, merged v0.7.0; Apache-2.0). Its README describes "bullish and bearish researchers who critically assess the insights … Through structured debates" and a "Risk Management and Portfolio Manager" team that makes the final decision. That project also includes analyst agents, debate-round settings and an academic paper, so it gives more than the DM prompts would.
- **Claims vs reality:**
  - "Most bots only hear one side": a rhetorical framing, not a measured claim.
  - "Every claim needs evidence": the post shows no mechanism for this. In LLM debates both sides can hallucinate "evidence" unless they are fed real data.
  - "No clear winner, no trade": a sensible conservative rule, but it is not tested here.
- **Honesty markers:** "SIMULATED SYSTEM", "NOT FINANCIAL ADVICE", "SIMULATE FIRST" and the caption's paper-trading disclaimer are present. No profit claim is made in this post.
- **Could not verify:** the contents of the DM prompts and setup guide (not engaged with, by design), and whether any working system exists behind the illustrations.

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
Soft CTA only: "Follow @chartfanatics to master trading". No product, link or price in this clip. Chart Fanatics is a trading-education brand (YouTube masterclasses, IG); the long-game funnel is audience-building toward its paid courses/community, but nothing is sold here. The information itself (SSRN exists and is free) is the substance.

### Verification & insights
- SSRN is genuinely free and is a standard source for quant/factor research; the advice "read the paper, extract why it should work, build your own rules, backtest, validate" is sound and matches how systematic desks actually prototype. Caveat: most published anomalies decay or weaken out-of-sample after publication (well-documented in the academic "factor zoo"/post-publication decay literature), and papers rarely include real-world costs, capacity or slippage.
- The "€30M / 90%" numbers are marketing hooks (title "€30 Million"); treat as unverified.
- The paper titles shown skew toward insider-trading/surveillance research (possibly chosen for on-screen drama) rather than classic tradeable factors, but they are real SSRN-style listings.
- Not verified: Matteo Conte's track record, the exact SSRN paper count, and any affiliate relationship.

## DdFryRpMyTD | @josholdmixon | Ex-hedge-fund quant explains pair trading: Coke vs Pepsi, cointegration vs correlation (rope analogy), mean-reversion entry/exit
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DdFryRpMyTD/
- **Account:** @josholdmixon (Josh Oldmixon, Founder & CEO of PentPort) · **Posted:** unknown (not in fetched data) · **Type:** reel (57s, 16 scene pages; 2-fps frames checked for the animations)
- **Caption source:** possibly truncated (mirror) – very short, likely complete
- **Category:** Quant & data
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE – correct basic explanation of pairs trading/cointegration; the "nearly half of all hedge funds" stat is overstated from a poll graphic, and one illustration is a single-asset Bollinger chart, not a pair spread

### Post description
> Pair trading! #quant

### Audio
Creator speaking English (to camera, holding a DJI mic):
- [0.0–7.9] Hook: "What if I told you that nearly half of all hedge funds use the same strategy? Well, according to Bloomberg, they do." It's pair trading, which was all he focused on as a hedge fund quant.
- [7.9–22.7] The common example is Coke and Pepsi: very similar companies with similar holdings. More soft-drink buying helps both, less hurts both, so when one moves a lot you'd expect the other to as well.
- [22.7–40.4] That's the idea behind cointegration, "not to be confused with correlation". Analogy: two people walking; with correlation they walk the same direction; with cointegration there's "a rope tied between them", so even if they drift apart the distance stays relatively constant.
- [40.4–53.2] This is the predictive part and why so many hedge funds use it: find two cointegrated assets and use mean reversion. When they move far enough apart, enter the trade, and profit when the relationship reverts to its mean.
- [53.2–57.8] He'll make this a series with more detail on pair trading; CTA: follow for more.

### On-screen content, page by page
Talking-head in a home office with monitors (one shows the green "PentPort" logo; hoodie printed "PentPort.com"); word-by-word subtitles.
**Page 1 (0–2s):** Title "Quant explains popular hedge fund strategy".
**Pages 2–3 (2–4.4s):** Pie chart "Do you trade pairs?" – "Yes, 47%" / "No, 53%"; "Source: Bloomberg Tradebook".
**Pages 4–5 (4.4–7.9s):** iPhone Messages search screenshot titled "'Pair trading' in Messages" (contents covered by a 🤫 shushing-face emoji).
**Pages 6–7 (7.9–14.6s):** Pepsi and Coca-Cola can images.
**Page 8 (14.6–22.7s):** Long-run price chart, Coca-Cola (pink) vs PepsiCo (red), x-axis 2000–'26, y-axis 0–300.
**Pages 9–11 (22.7–28s):** Talking head ("This is cointegration, not to be confused with correlation").
**Page 12 (28–40s):** Animation: red and blue stick figures walking; first apart on diverging dashed paths (correlation), then joined by a rope so the gap stays constant (cointegration).
**Pages 13–15 (40–53s):** Chart with Bollinger-style bands labelled "Upper 2 SD", "Upper 1 SD", "Midline", "Lower 1 SD", "Lower 2 SD", "Trailing Stop Loss", arrows "Enter Long" (twice) and "Exit".
**Page 16 (53–57s):** Talking head; ends with "Follow for more!".

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pair trading | strategy | caption, audio, title | Real, long-established market-neutral stat-arb strategy (Gatev, Goetzmann & Rouwenhorst, NBER w7032) |
| "Nearly half of all hedge funds use the same strategy… according to Bloomberg" / "Do you trade pairs? Yes 47% / No 53%, Source: Bloomberg Tradebook" | claim/number | audio 0–4s, pages 2–3 | Source survey not found. The graphic is a poll answer ("Do you trade pairs?"), probably from a Bloomberg Tradebook client/webinar audience, not a census of all hedge funds, so "nearly half of all hedge funds" overstates it |
| Coke (KO) and Pepsi (PEP) | tickers | audio 8–22s, pages 6–8 | Classic textbook pair example |
| Cointegration vs correlation | concept | audio 22–40s, page 12 | Explanation is correct: cointegration means the spread is stationary (mean-reverting); correlation only means co-movement of returns. The rope analogy is a version of the well-known "drunk and her dog" analogy (Murray 1994) |
| Mean reversion entry/exit | rule | audio 43–53s | Enter when the spread is far from its mean, exit when it reverts. No thresholds given (commonly a ±2σ z-score entry and exit near 0) |
| Upper/Lower 1 SD & 2 SD, Enter Long, Exit, Trailing Stop Loss chart | graphic | pages 13–15 | A single-instrument Bollinger-band chart, not a pair spread; illustrative only |
| PentPort / pentport.com | site/company | monitor and hoodie in every frame | "Trade. Compete. Get Paid." Free paper-trading competitions/leagues, API and Python package; says it is building a brokerage "that shares order flow revenue with traders". Team page: Josh Oldmixon, Founder & CEO, "background in quantitative finance… experience working across multiple hedge funds" |
| "I was a hedge fund quant" | claim | audio 5–8s | Consistent with his PentPort bio (no fund names given; not independently verified) |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No explicit CTA beyond "follow for more" (a promised series). Implicit promotion: the creator's startup **PentPort** (logo on monitor, URL on hoodie) – free paper-trading competitions now, with a planned order-flow-revenue-sharing brokerage. No price or affiliate link shown.

### Verification & insights
- pentport.com and /team fetched: confirms Josh Oldmixon is Founder & CEO; co-founder Ethan DuBrueler (Chief AI Officer); advisors include Tom Dorsey (founder of Dorsey Wright & Associates, acquired by Nasdaq in 2015) and Tom Walker. Site claims 5,000+ traders.
- Accuracy: the conceptual content is right. Caveats the reel leaves out: cointegration relationships break down (regime change), so real implementations need stop rules and periodic re-testing (Engle-Granger/Johansen); returns have compressed since the 1990s as the trade got crowded; costs include two-legged commissions, borrow fees on the short leg, and slippage.
- KO vs PEP is the standard example, but it is often *not* reliably cointegrated over long windows. The page-8 chart shows them drifting apart at times, which illustrates the risk.
- Not verified: the Bloomberg Tradebook 47% poll (no source found), the creator's hedge-fund employment details.

- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DerzqqviEq4/
- **Account:** @neuron.machinelearning (NEURON) · **Posted:** 2026-10-19 (decoded from the post ID; not in metadata) · **Type:** carousel (5 slides)

- **Category:** Quant & data (ML education)
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE. Correct, standard ML fundamentals. The numbers check out arithmetically.

### Post description
> No single equation trains a neural network. These 4 do it together. 🧠⚡
>
> 	1.	The Neuron: a weighted vote + a squash
> 	2.	The Loss: punishes confident mistakes hardest
> 	3.	Gradient Descent: step downhill, η decides everything
> 	4.	Backprop: the chain rul...

(truncated by the mirror; the rest could not be fetched)

### Audio
None (carousel).

### On-screen content, page by page
Every slide has "NEURON" at top left, "n / 5" at top right, "@neuron.machinelearning" at bottom left and "real runs · numpy" at bottom right.
**Slide 1 (cover):** "4 equations that **TRAIN EVERY NEURAL NETWORK**". Floating cards: `a = σ(wᵀx + b)` · `θ ← θ − η∇θL` · `L = −log pθ(y | x)` · `∂L/∂W₁ = ∏ₗ ∂aₗ/∂aₗ₋₁ …`. Thumbnails of the slide 4 loss chart and the slide 5 gradient chart. "swipe →".
**Slide 2: "1. THE NEURON".** "Multiply every input by a weight, add a bias, squash the result into a curve. That is one neuron: a weighted vote followed by a soft yes/no. Training only ever changes w and b. Deep learning is millions of these, stacked." Formula `z = wᵀx + b,  a = σ(z) = 1/(1 + e^(−z))`. Two scatter plots with one linear boundary: "two clusters: **100.0% accuracy**" | "XOR: **48.0% accuracy**". "It breaks on anything that is not a straight line. One neuron draws exactly one boundary, so on XOR it scored 48%, worse than a coin flip. That is why hidden layers exist."
**Slide 3: "2. THE LOSS".** "The network gives every class a probability. Cross-entropy looks at one number only: the probability it gave the right answer, and takes its negative log. 90% on the truth costs 0.11. 1% on the truth costs 4.61, 44× more." Formula `L(θ) = −(1/N) Σᵢ₌₁ᴺ log pθ(yᵢ | xᵢ)`. Chart of loss vs "probability the model gives the TRUE class" (0–1), points: p=0.01 → loss 4.61 · p=0.1 → loss 2.30 · p=0.5 → loss 0.69 · p=0.9 → loss 0.11 (the last two labels overlap on the image). "It breaks on wrong labels. A mislabeled example looks exactly like a confident mistake, so it gets the biggest loss and pulls hardest on the weights."
**Slide 4: "3. GRADIENT DESCENT".** "Compute which direction makes the loss go up, step the other way, repeat. The step size η is the knob. Same network, same XOR data, 300 steps, three learning rates, three different outcomes." Formula `θₜ₊₁ = θₜ − η∇θL(θₜ)`. Chart: y "loss (XOR, 16 hidden)" 0–10, x "step" 0–300. Legend: η = 0.01 → final loss 0.60 (grey, slow) · η = 1 → final loss 0.07 (orange) · η = 150 → final loss 0.70 (red, spikes up to about 10 then noisy around 1). "It breaks at the extremes. η = 0.01 crawls and is still at loss 0.60. η = 150 overshoots and keeps bouncing, ending at 0.70. η = 1 reached 0.07."
**Slide 5: "4. BACKPROP".** "How did a weight in layer 1 change the loss? Multiply the local slopes of every layer after it. That is the chain rule run backwards, and it hands every weight its gradient in a single pass." Formula `∂L/∂W₁ = (∂L/∂a_L) ∏ₗ₌₂ᴸ (∂aₗ/∂aₗ₋₁) (∂a₁/∂W₁)`. Log chart: y "mean |gradient|" 10⁻⁹ to 10⁻², x "layer (1 = closest to the input)" 1–11. Sigmoid rises from about 1e-9 (layer 1) to about 1.3e-2 (layer 11). ReLU is flat at about 1.2e-3 to 1.5e-3, about 5e-3 at layer 11. "It breaks when the slopes are small. Sigmoid's slope is at most 0.25, so in a 10-layer net on real MNIST, layer 1 got a gradient **12.7 million times smaller** than the output layer. With ReLU: **4.6×**."

### Everything named or linked
| Item | Type | Where it appeared | What it really is (verified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sigmoid neuron `a = σ(wᵀx+b)` | equation | slides 1–2 | Standard logistic neuron |
| XOR 48% / two clusters 100% | numbers | slide 2 | Plausible. A single linear boundary can't solve XOR (Minsky and Papert 1969), so about 50% is expected. |
| Cross-entropy values | numbers | slide 3 | Correct: −ln 0.9 = 0.105, −ln 0.5 = 0.693, −ln 0.1 = 2.303, −ln 0.01 = 4.605; 4.605 / 0.105 ≈ 44× |
| Gradient descent, η = 0.01 / 1 / 150 | parameters | slide 4 | Standard update rule. Final losses 0.60 / 0.07 / 0.70 are the creator's run (not reproduced). |
| Backprop chain rule; sigmoid max slope 0.25 | equation/number | slide 5 | Correct (σ′(z) = σ(1−σ) ≤ 0.25 at z = 0). The vanishing-gradient result is the classic Hochreiter/Glorot finding. |
| MNIST, 10-layer net, 12.7M× and 4.6× | numbers | slide 5 | Consistent with the plotted values (about 1e-9 vs about 1.3e-2 ≈ 10⁷; ReLU about 1.2e-3 vs about 5.5e-3 ≈ 4.6×). Not re-run. The plot shows 11 layer indices (10 hidden plus output). |
| NEURON / @neuron.machinelearning | brand | all slides | ML-education account. Its Instagram bio (per a prior review, DeQuUyPo8pR) points to a free "Practice Machine Learning" web app. |

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
No call to action on the slides. The visible caption has no comment keyword. The account funnels to its free NEURON practice web app in the bio (no paid tier found in prior reviews). The post itself sells nothing.

### Verification & insights
- All four equations and every arithmetic claim I could check are correct. Experiment numbers ("real runs · numpy") are plausible but not executed – unverified.
- Small nitpicks: "worse than a coin flip" at 48% is within noise of 50% on a few hundred points. "Training only ever changes w and b" holds for this neuron but not for layers like batch-norm (which have their own learned parameters).
- The same material, free and in more depth: 3Blue1Brown's "Neural networks" series, Karpathy's "micrograd"/"Zero to Hero", Nielsen's "Neural Networks and Deep Learning" (online book; chapter 5 covers vanishing gradients).
- Part of a series by the same account (see DeQuUyPo8pR, DeRNXXMoH--, DerOLxFoAIu, DesSEnqI0tk, Derz_uNoyCl).

### Audio

### On-screen content, page by page
Each slide is a 4.5s animation in which the charts draw in. Every course slide ends with a "WHERE TO WATCH" bar and a "QUANT REALITY" box.

- **Slide 1 (cover).** "01 / 08 · FREE QUANT LECTURES". There is an "EFFICIENT FRONTIER" chart (expected return 0–0.15 vs risk/volatility 0–0.3). Headline: "7 QUANT FINANCE LECTURES YOU CAN ACCESS FREE ONLINE". The list:
  1. MIT Finance Theory I

  Footnote: "Free on OCW / YouTube / edX; Coursera courses may need audit or trial access. Availability can change. Chart: illustrative."
- **Slide 2, "LECTURE 1 OF 7": "1. MIT FINANCIAL THEORY I · FOUNDATION OF MODERN FINANCE".** "This course introduces the core models and principles that shape financial markets and investment decisions — tools that remain the bedrock of quantitative finance."
  - On the board:
    - Black-Scholes option pricing: C = S·N(d1) − K·e^(−rT)·N(d2), with d2 = d1 − σ√T and d1 = [ln(S/K) + (r + ½σ²)T] / (σ√T)
    - CAPM: E[Ri] = Rf + βi(E[Rm] − Rf)
    - Mean-variance: min_w wᵀΣw s.t. μᵀw = μp, 1ᵀw = 1
  - Chart: "CAPM · SECURITY MARKET LINE", with expected return 0–16% vs beta 0–2, Rf = 2%, and "slope = market risk premium".
  - Tiles: BLACK-SCHOLES "Options pricing model used across derivatives markets"; CAPM "Measures expected return relative to systematic risk"; MEAN VARIANCE "Foundation of portfolio optimisation and efficient frontier theory".
  - Where to watch: MIT OpenCourseWare · 15.401 Finance Theory I (Andrew Lo).
  - Quant reality: "Most modern quant finance starts here. Black-Scholes, CAPM and portfolio optimisation remain core concepts at trading desks, hedge funds and asset managers."
- **Slide 3, "LECTURE 2 OF 7": "2. MIT STATISTICS FOR APPLICATIONS · STATISTICAL THINKING FOR QUANTS".** "Master the statistical tools that turn data into insights. From modelling relationships to making inferences, these concepts underpin the quantitative decisions you'll make."
  - On the board:
    - Simple linear regression Yi = β0 + β1Xi + εi, εi ~ N(0, σ²)
    - Hypothesis test H0: θ = θ0 vs H1: θ ≠ θ0
    - Test statistic Z = (θ̂ − θ0)/SE(θ̂)
    - p-value p = P(|Z| ≥ |z_obs|), reject H0 if p < α
  - Chart: "REGRESSION · SYNTHETIC SAMPLE", Ŷ = 1.94 + 0.75X.
  - Tiles: REGRESSION, PROBABILITY, HYPOTHESIS TESTING.
  - Where to watch: MIT OpenCourseWare · 18.650 Statistics for Applications (Philippe Rigollet).
  - Quant reality: "Great quants think statistically. Sound decisions come from solid data and rigorous testing."
- **Slide 4, "LECTURE 3 OF 7": "3. COLUMBIA · FINANCIAL ENGINEERING · STOCHASTIC MODELLING".** "Learn the mathematical framework behind modern derivatives pricing and quantitative models: stochastic processes and continuous-time modelling."
  - On the board:
    - GBM: dSt = μSt dt + σSt dWt
    - Solution: St = S0 exp((μ − ½σ²)t + σWt)
    - Itô's lemma: df = (ft + μS fS + ½σ²S² fSS)dt + σS fS dW
    - Black-Scholes PDE: V(S,T) = Φ(S); Vt + ½σ²S²VSS + rSVS − rV = 0
  - Chart: "GBM PATHS · μ=7%, σ=22%", price 70–160 over 0–1y, dashed E[St] = S0·e^(μt).
  - Tiles: ITÔ CALCULUS, MONTE CARLO ("Simulate thousands of scenarios to price path-dependent products"), DERIVATIVES.
  - Where to watch: Coursera · Financial Engineering & Risk Management (Columbia) · "audit options vary".
  - Quant reality: "Stochastic calculus turns randomness into models, and models into prices."
- **Slide 5, "LECTURE 4 OF 7": "4. STANFORD · PROBABILITY & STATISTICS · UNCERTAINTY & PREDICTION".** "Understand randomness. Make better predictions. This course builds the statistical intuition and rigour needed to extract signals from noisy, uncertain data."
  - On the board: Bayes' theorem; law of total probability P(B) = Σ P(B|Ai)P(Ai); normal pdf; CLT X̄n ≈ N(μ, σ²/n).
  - Chart: "CLT · MEANS OF N EXP(1) DRAWS", n = 1, 5, 30, with dashed N(1, 1/30).
  - Tiles: BAYESIAN INFERENCE, MARKOV CHAINS, PREDICTIVE MODELS.
  - Where to watch: YouTube · Stanford CS109 Probability for Computer Scientists.
  - Quant reality: "Better models. Better predictions. Better decisions. Probability is the edge in an uncertain world."
- **Slide 6, "LECTURE 5 OF 7": "5. UNIVERSITY OF NAPLES FEDERICO II · MARKET MICROSTRUCTURE · HOW TRADING REALLY WORKS".** "Explore how markets work beneath the surface: order books, trading costs, liquidity and price impact — and how algorithmic and high-frequency trading have reshaped markets."
  - On the board:
    - Mid price m = (bid+ask)/2; spread s = ask − bid
    - Square-root impact (empirical rule of thumb): Impact ≈ Yσ(Q/ADV)^β with β ≈ 0.5, where σ = volatility, Q = order size and ADV = average daily volume
  - Chart: "LIMIT ORDER BOOK DEPTH", with cumulative size 0–10k, price 99.95–100.10 (illustrative), and mid 100.045.
  - Tiles: ORDER BOOKS, EXECUTION, LIQUIDITY ("…how fast liquidity disappears in stress").
  - Where to watch: Coursera · Market Microstructure (Marco Pagano) · audit options vary.
  - Quant reality: "Even a good trading signal can lose money if execution costs and market impact are ignored."
- **Slide 7, "LECTURE 6 OF 7": "6. MIT 18.S096 · MATHEMATICS IN FINANCE · THE LANGUAGE OF DERIVATIVES".** "Derivatives are priced using advanced mathematics. This course teaches the equations and models that describe how financial instruments behave in uncertain markets."
  - On the board:
    - Black-Scholes equation
    - Feynman-Kac: V = E^Q[e^(−r(T−t))Φ(ST) | St = S]
    - Itô's lemma
    - Girsanov: dWt^Q = dWt + θt dt
  - Chart: "IMPLIED-VOL SURFACE · ILLUSTRATIVE" (axes implied vol, strike, maturity).
  - Tiles: PDES (∂V/∂t + 𝓛V = 0), SDES, VOLATILITY SURFACES.
  - Where to watch: MIT OpenCourseWare · 18.S096 Topics in Mathematics with Applications in Finance.
  - Quant reality: "Mathematics turns financial intuition into precise models — the equations behind pricing a wide range of instruments."
- **Slide 8, "LECTURE 7 OF 7": "7. HARVARD STAT 110 · PROBABILITY · CORE OF QUANT RESEARCH".** "Probability is the foundation of quantitative models. This course develops the theory and intuition behind randomness, dependencies and distributions, so you can model uncertainty with precision."
  - On the board: law of total probability; Bayes; E[X] = Σ x·P(X=x); Var(X) = E[X²] − (E[X])².
  - Chart: "COMMON DISTRIBUTIONS": N(0,1), Pois(λ = 3), Exp(λ = 1).
  - Tiles: BAYES, POISSON, CONDITIONAL PROBABILITY.
  - Where to watch: YouTube + edX · Stat 110 Probability (Joe Blitzstein) · free textbook online.
  - Quant reality: "Quant models are built on probability. Master it, and you can model uncertainty almost anywhere."

### What's being promoted / the funnel decoded
"Comment 'Quant'" gets a DM that presumably holds links to the seven courses. Every one can be found directly by the course names above, so there is no need to comment. A web search found no info on @quantguy_usa (Mehul Mehta) or any product. No paid offer is visible in the post. It is an engagement or follower-growth carousel.

### Verification & insights
- I checked every formula by reading. Black-Scholes, d1/d2, CAPM, the mean-variance program, the GBM solution, Itô, the BS PDE, Feynman-Kac, Girsanov, Bayes, CLT and Var are all stated correctly. The square-root impact law is correctly labelled as an empirical rule of thumb.
- Charts are labelled "illustrative" or "synthetic"; they are teaching visuals, not data.
- **Course availability:** OCW and YouTube are free. Coursera courses are free to audit in most cases (certificates paid), and the post itself discloses this.
- These are standard, well-known recommendations, essentially the canonical free quant curriculum. Nothing misleading was found.

### Verification & insights
- **Repos:** qlib, FinRL, tensortrade, Lean and vectorbt were cloned shallow (or reused) in `scratchpad/repos/`, with LICENSE and last commit read. Backtrader, freqtrade, zipline and catalyst were checked with `git ls-remote`. Star counts come from the GitHub pages. The slide's counts are all far too low, which points to an AI-generated image.
- **Claims vs reality:**
  - "Free" is true for the software and basic Colab. The headline promise "find winning strategies" is not supported. A RandomForest on next-day returns with a `predictions > 0` long/flat rule is a textbook toy that usually fails after costs.
  - The slides themselves list the right safeguards (fees and slippage, out-of-sample, walk-forward, benchmark), but they show a mock +312% result instead of any real test.
  - "Buy (1) / Sell (0)" in the code is really long/flat, not short.
- **Risks:** overfitting and data snooping (see DeWlPe2id2c in this batch, where the best of 498 rules on noise made +11% in-sample and −28% out-of-sample). yfinance is unofficial and can break.
- Could not verify the contents of the "ML" DM.

## DeY9P8_CpfW | @quantfinancetogo | "Quant Finance From Scratch, Part 21: Linear Regression": a simulated stock-vs-market regression (beta 1.26, alpha +0.05%/day, R² 52%) with live Python code
- **Link:** https://www.instagram.com/p/DeY9P8_CpfW/
- **Account:** @quantfinancetogo · **Posted:** 2026-10-12 (decoded from the shortcode) · **Type:** reel (105.1s; animated explainer, 2 scene pages + continuous animation reviewed at 2 fps)
- **Caption source:** possibly truncated (mirror)
- **Category:** Quant & data
- **Verdict:** LEGIT & FREE. A correct, honest explainer of OLS regression and beta, with reproducible code. Every on-screen number reproduced exactly when I re-ran the shown code (it is my own transcription of the numpy snippet). It is labeled "simulated market, assumed numbers · education only · not financial advice".

### Post description
> ➡️ Quantitative Finance From Scratch, Part 21: Linear Regression in finance
>
> Linear regression finds the straight line that best describes how one quantity moves with another. In finance, that is often a stock's daily return against the market's dai...

(Cut off by the mirror. The rest was not retrievable because Instagram returned 429.)

### Audio
Narration by the creator (synthetic-sounding voice), condensed into timestamped notes:
- [0.0] Hook: "This one line tells you how much of a stock's move comes from the market factor. It's called linear regression."
- [5.0] Series title: Quantitative finance from scratch, part 21.
- [7.4] Linear regression finds the straight line that best describes how one quantity moves with another; in finance often a stock's daily return against the market's.
- [16.6] "Best" means smallest squared errors: for each day, measure the distance of the real return from the line, square it and sum; the line minimising this sum wins (least squares).
- [31.4] Two numbers: the slope (how strongly the stock follows the market, the beta from part 14) and the intercept, alpha (average earnings when the market does not move).
- [43.8] The unexplained part is the residual (the move unrelated to the market). R squared gives the share of the swings the line explains, from 0 (nothing) to 1 (everything).
- [57.4] Result: "In our simulated market, the line finds a beta of 1.26, close to the 1.2 we built in, and it explains 52% of the swings."
- [65.4] With two inputs the line becomes a plane, explaining a return with the market and a second factor at once: the idea behind factor models.
- [73.8] Quants use regression to estimate betas, size hedges, test whether a strategy earns more than its risk, and check whether two assets really move together.
- [83.4] Limits: a regression only finds a relationship in the data given; "It does not prove that one thing causes the other." A few extreme days can pull the line, and past relationships can change.
- [94.0] Summary: the best line through the data; slope, intercept and R squared tell how much of a move the market explains.
- [101.4] CTA: "Follow to really learn quantitative finance."

### On-screen content, page by page
Constant frame: "QUANT FINANCE FROM SCRATCH ▌ PART 21 / **Linear Regression** / the best line through the data", a series bar 13–21 with 21 highlighted, and the footer "simulated market, assumed numbers · education only · not financial advice".

- **0–5s:** Formula **R_stock = α + β·R_market + ε**. A 3D scatter ("stock" from −4% to +4% vs "market" from −2% to +2%; "504 simulated days · each dot is one day"). β animates (0.46 → 0.81 …) while R² reads 0%.
- **~5–7s:** Title card "QUANTITATIVE" with a fan of simulated price paths.
- **~10–25s:** "one dot = one day · market vs stock". Labels: "Y: THE STOCK, DAILY RETURN", "X: THE MARKET, DAILY RETURN", with an example dot "ONE DAY: MARKET +1.1% / STOCK +2.0%". The code editor `regression.py` starts typing.
- **~25–35s:** "least squares · sum of squared errors". Residual sticks are drawn, an "ADD EVERYTHING UP" bar runs, and a parabola of the sum of squares vs slope is labeled "**MINIMUM AT 1.26**".
- **~35–45s:** "slope = beta · intercept = alpha". Callouts "SLOPE BETA = 1.26 / BETA FROM PART 14" and "**ALPHA +0.05% A DAY**", with a rise/run triangle (1% → 1.26%).
- **~45–57s:** "residual and R² · explained vs not". "RESIDUAL: WHAT THE LINE MISSES / NOTHING TO DO WITH THE MARKET". Bar "swings of the stock: 48% / **52%**", then "**R² = 52%**".
- **~57–65s:** "our run · simulated, seed 21". Terminal: `$ python regression.py` → "**line: beta 1.26, alpha +0.05%, R² 52%**". A "beta found" slider runs 1.0–1.5.
- **~65–73s:** "two inputs: a plane" (`plane.py`). A 3D plane fit labeled "**MARKET BETA 1.27**", "**FACTOR BETA 0.60**", "**R² = 63%**" (it briefly shows 55% while animating).
- **~73–83s:** "what quants use it for · numbers from our run". Four panels:
  - ESTIMATE BETAS (β = 1.26)
  - SIZE HEDGES ("stock alone **1.64%**" vs "minus 1.26 × market **1.14%**", daily swings)
  - BEAT THE RISK? ("alpha +0.05%", with a caption that zero lies in the range of chance)
  - MOVE TOGETHER? (two overlaid price paths)
- **~83–94s:** "the limits · what a line cannot tell". "ONLY THE DATA YOU GIVE IT" (dashed extrapolation) and "THREE INVENTED EXTREME DAYS" (three outlier dots pulling the line).
- **~94–105s:** Recap "linear regression · part 21": "SLOPE BETA = 1.26", "INTERCEPT ALPHA = +0.05%", "R² = 52%", "explained by the market 52%".

The final code on screen (`regression.py`, 13 lines), excerpt:
```
rng = np.random.default_rng(21)
days = 252 * 2                           # two simulated years
market = rng.normal(0, .01, days)        # daily returns
stock = 1.2 * market + noise + .6 * factor
x, y = market - market.mean(), stock - stock.mean()
beta = (x * y).sum() / (x * x).sum()     # the slope
alpha = stock.mean() - beta * market.mean()
resid = y - beta * x                     # what the line misses
r2 = 1 - (resid**2).sum() / (y**2).sum()
print(f"line: beta {beta:.2f}, alpha {alpha:+.2%}, R² {r2:.0%}")
```
The omitted lines import numpy and draw `noise` ("its own swings") and `factor` ("a 2nd factor"), each `rng.normal(0, .01, days)`. Inputs: seed 21, 504 days, true beta 1.2, factor loading 0.6. Printed result: "line: beta 1.26, alpha +0.05%, R² 52%".

`plane.py`: `from regression import *` · `X = np.c_[market, factor, np.ones(days)]` · `fit = np.linalg.lstsq(X, stock)[0]  # least squares` · `resid = stock - X @ fit` · `r2 = 1 - (resid**2).sum() / (y**2).sum()` · `print(f"plane: betas {fit[0]:.2f} and {fit[1]:.2f}, R² {r2:.0%}")`.

### Verification & insights
